# Hidden Heroes Behind Chip Testing; Initiate Hon Precision, MPI and WinWay at OW

April 16, 2026 09:01 PM GMT Semiconductor testing is entering a multi-year structural upcycle 订阅研报添加微信 LCD123321Cdriven by AI demand, rising chip complexity and larger package sizes, and higher testing intensity. We see upside potential in the segment over the long term, with MPI, WinWay, and Hon Precision relatively well positioned to benefit.

AI Driving Structural Upswing in Semiconductor Testing: We see a structural inflection in semiconductor testing as chip complexity rises at advanced nodes and as product cycles shorten. As AI and high-performance computing (HPC) push performance limits, testing intensity and duration continue to increase, elevating advanced testing from a cost consideration to a critical bottleneck and value driver. Taiwan-based vendors remain well positioned, given their proximity to TSMC, ASE and KYEC, which enables faster iteration cycles and closer customer integration. We expect sustained growth in testing content per chip to underpin long-term industry expansion across the semiconductor testing ecosystem.

订阅研报添加微信 LCD123321CContent Growth, Share Gains and CPO to Drive Upside; MPI and WinWay at OW with respective PTs at NT\$5,800 and NT\$12,000: TSMC targets interposer sizes of up to 9.7 reticles by 2027, vs 1.5 reticles in 2016. As package sizes expand and testing complexity increases, we see compelling opportunities in probe cards and sockets, supported by rising dollar content per unit, market share gains, and TAM expansion. Considering MPI and WinWay are leaders in the probe card and socket markets, we forecast revenue CAGRs of $6 6 \%$ and $7 1 \%$ , 2025-28E, respectively, driven by AI and HPC demand, where higher pin counts, faster signal speeds, and tighter tolerances are driving step function increases in ASPs.

AI Testing Handler Leader with Underappreciated Upside; Hon Precision at OW with PT at $\yen 123,456,7$ : With $> 7 0 \%$ share in ${ \mathsf { A l / H P C } }$ final-test handlers, it is well positioned to benefit from rising testing intensity and longer test cycles for next-gen AI chips. We forecast a $6 \%$ revenue CAGR, 2025-28e, supported by strong AI/HPC demand. Leadership in active thermal control (ATC) should drive content expansion 订阅研报添加微信 LCD123321Caand ASP upside, while emerging opportunities such as micro-channel lids (MCL) and co-packaged optics (CPO) should support margin expansion toward ${ \sim } 6 1 \%$ by 2028e.

Key risks to our outlook and stocks include: 1) slower AI demand, which could weaken growth given reliance on hyperscaler spending; 2) supply and infrastructure constraints, such as limited fab capacity and power shortages, which may delay deployment; 3) intensifying competition, potentially pressuring market share and margins; and 4) slower adoption of new technologies (e.g., CPO, mcl SLT), which could defer incremental revenue upside.

# Executive Summary

We believe investors should increasingly focus on the semiconductor testing market now, as the industry is undergoing a structural inflection driven by AI, HPC, and advanced packaging. Unlike prior cycles where testing was viewed as a cost center, rising chip complexity (chiplets, HBM, 2.5D/3D packaging) is materially increasing test intensity, duration, and criticality, turning testing into a bottleneck for performance and yield. AI accelerators in particular require significantly longer and more complex testing (e.g., HBM 添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 testing 5–10x longer than traditional DRAM), while emerging technologies such as CPO and system-level testing (SLT) add incremental test steps. This drives both volume growth and non-linear increases in content per chip, creating, in our view, a structurally attractive, underpenetrated growth opportunity across handlers, probe cards, and sockets.

Against this backdrop, we initiate Overweight (OW) ratings on Hon Precision, MPI, and WinWay, as all three are key enablers of this structural shift and are positioned to benefit from both AI-driven demand and rising testing intensity. Importantly, our estimates across all three names are generally above consensus, reflecting our view that the market is underappreciating the durability of AI-driven test demand, the magnitude of ASP expansion, and the incremental upside from new test insertions (SLT, CPO, burn-in).

# Hon Precision (OW): AI Testing Intensity – Underappreciated Earnings Upside

• We rate Hon Precision Overweight given its dominant position in AI/HPC test handlers ${ \sim } 7 0 \%$ share) and direct leverage to rising test time per chip. As AI chips   
加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 become larger and more power-dense, thermal management and testing duration are increasing significantly, driving both higher tool demand and ASP expansion via its ATC technology. In addition, new opportunities such as SLT, CPO (Insertion 4), and micro-channel lid testing further expand its addressable market.   
• Crucially, our forecasts are above consensus, with 2026/2027 EPS estimates ${ \sim } 1 0 \% / 2 0 \% +$ higher, driven by stronger AI demand, higher tool intensity, and earlier monetization of new test steps. We believe the market still underestimates the non-linear impact of longer test times, which can drive both higher utilization and incremental equipment demand, supporting a structurally higher growth and margin profile.

# MPI (OW): Probe Card Technology Leader with CPO Optionality

• We rate MPI Overweight as a key beneficiary of rising probe card complexity, particularly in MEMS and high-density VPC solutions required for AI and advanced 加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 nodes. Increasing pin counts, higher current requirements, and high-speed signal integrity needs are driving a structural upgrade cycle in probe cards, where MPI is gaining share. Its equipment segment also provides early-stage customer engagement and CPO-related upside, enhancing long-term growth optionality. Our estimates are meaningfully above consensus, particularly in outer years (e.g., 2027 EPS ${\sim } 3 0 \% +$ above), reflecting faster adoption of MEMS probe cards, improving mix, and incremental contribution from CPO testing. We believe consensus underestimates both structural ASP uplift from higher pin density and MPI’s ability to expand beyond traditional probe cards into higher-value testing solutions.

# WinWay (OW): Socket Leader Leveraging AI Packaging Complexity

• We rate WinWay Overweight given its leadership in test sockets and strong exposure to AI-driven increases in package size, pin count, and testing complexity. As chips scale toward $> 1 0 \boldsymbol { \mathrm { k } }$ pins and higher power requirements, sockets become critical for signal integrity and reliability, driving migration toward higher-end solutions such as coaxial sockets and hypersockets, which carry structurally higher ASPs and margins.

• Our forecasts are also above consensus (2026/2027 EPS \~10–20% higher), 加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 supported by faster adoption of hypersockets, rising SLT and burn-in demand, and improving product mix. We believe the market is underappreciating the margin inflection potential from hypersockets and the long-term earnings leverage from vertical integration and probe card expansion, positioning WinWay for sustained outperformance.

# Chip Testing Process and the Roles of Hon Precision, WinWay and MPI in the Semi Supply Chain

Testing process for semiconductor electrical testing: Semiconductor testers (electrical testing) verify that devices operate as designed. Precise control and analysis of input and output signals allow testers to identify functional and defective units, grade products by noise level (electronic signal fluctuations), and analyze defects. Test results support 添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 performance improvements, shorten development cycles, enhance manufacturing quality, and improve yields.

![](images/50149bfc7c986bfb3c14e01dc2db12949ea4b62f1969d84d542e2d25380954e7.jpg)  
Exhibit 12: The role of semiconductor testers   
Source: Morgan Stanley Research.

Tester categories: Testers determine whether semiconductor devices are fault-free or defective. Semiconductor testing includes wafer probing, which tests wafers before dicing, and final testing of completed devices after packaging. These stages use one or more of the following test types: DC testing, functional testing, and AC (timing compliance) testing ( Exhibit 13 ).

![](images/7c67811925492b7b4e58ad34fb706e24e1ac1bdb12cf90a30ddcbc32953d9d6e.jpg)  
Exhibit 13: Types of semiconductor testing   
Source: Morgan Stanley Research.

Different testers for different devices: Semiconductor testers include DRAM testers, flash memory testers, SoC testers, logic testers, LCD driver IC testers, CMOS/CCD center testers, and analog testers, depending on the device being tested. In this report, we classify testers into two categories: memory testers and SoC testers (non-memory testers).

![](images/e2a83dcf51f8da55a309cef392aad2a0cf95bfab9971fac01fa96f846d69873b.jpg)  
Exhibit 14: Test program creation: Testing flow for mass production devices   
Source: Advantest.

DC parametric testing checks basic functioning: DC parametric testing applies current to verify whether a device produces the specified output voltage, applies voltage to verify whether it produces the specified current, and checks for open circuits and electrical shorts. This represents the most basic form of semiconductor testing.

Functional testing checks device functionality: Functional testing assesses whether a device operates as specified. It verifies the ability of logic circuits to process digital data (calculation) and of memory devices to read and write data. This represents the most time intensive testing process.

添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 Checking for mutual interference takes time: For memory devices, this testing process assesses not only whether the individual memory cells function correctly, but also pattern dependence, storage time, potential interference from neighboring cells, and read/write speed. Functionality testing applies predefined test patterns ( Exhibit 15 ), which consist address sequences used to access memory location and data written into the memory cells. The industry has developed a wide range of test patterns to enable efficient defect detection.

![](images/6e9cd6aeadecf7f18ca8828c70c35c8c20e5ea13a6eefe971cabdcf234aedffc.jpg)  
Exhibit 15: Test pattern   
Source: Morgan Stanley Research.

Test pattern revision to reduce test duration: The test pattern required to check N cells typically involves between ${ \mathsf { N } } { \boldsymbol { \Lambda } } { \mathsf { 1 . 5 } }$ and $\mathsf { N } \wedge 2$ steps. Consequently, as memory density has increased, testing time has risen sharply. In response, device designers have increasingly developed proprietary test patterns tailored to their specific products, rather than relying on conventional ${ \mathsf { N } } \wedge 1 . 5 - { \mathsf { N } } \wedge 2$ step patterns that entail lengthy testing and evaluation cycles. CPUs and other logic devices integrate a broad range of functions, making careful test pattern design vital to achieving sufficient coverage within a limited testing timeframe.

Highly complex AC parametric testing to verify high-speed signal waveform: AC parametric testing evaluates output signal waveform. In digital circuits, the output signal represents either 0 or 1. The signal should register as 1 when the output voltage exceeds a defined threshold value and as 0 when the output voltage does not. AC parametric testing verifies that the voltage exceeds the threshold value when the signal is 1 and remains below it when the signal is 0. It also checks whether the signal transitions from 0 to 1 within the specified timeframe, identifies timing mismatches across different pins, and detects waveform timing errors at individual pins.

订阅研报

![](images/cce5121a044c43dee8ade2a08e5c36ef2af8e3a6073475f1e1fccbec2960b87c.jpg)  
Source: Semiconductor Equipment Association of Japan (SEAJ).

Faster device speeds make testing more difficult: Memory and logic devices have rapidly shifted toward higher operating speeds, significantly increasing the complexity of output waveform timing verification. This trend has intensified competition between tester manufacturers and driven tester prices higher.

Probe testing and final testing: As noted earlier, probe testing ( Exhibit 17 ) tests individual chips on a wafer. The wafer is mounted on a wafer prober, and pins on the probe card connected to the tester make contact with the bonding pads on each chip. By contrast, final testing ( Exhibit 18 ) takes place after burn-in to screen for early-life failure once device assembly is complete. In this stage, a handler picks up the device under test from a storage tray and inserts it into a test socket connected to the test head.

Noise is the enemy in probe testing: Probe testing relies on probe cards and wafer probes, which can introduce electrical signal noise and signal variation across pins. As a result, certain AC parametric tests – particularly waveform testing for high-speed processors– as well as portions of functional testing, are typically performed during final testing..

![](images/d3b0cc6f51357205b8cddcc68eeae370c3f4498680c21c9af33e5b0d47423138.jpg)  
Exhibit 17: Probe testing (tester $^ +$ wafer prober $^ +$ probe cards)   
Source: Morgan Stanley Research.

![](images/fdfdc50cd862de7e68a7caa1f725baa0ecc1661a228ec8a5695094c0aee546aa.jpg)  
Exhibit 18: Final testing (tester $^ +$ handler)   
Source: Morgan Stanley Research.

# #1: Sizing the AI TAM and Semi Test Equipment and Consumable Market

We expect the global AI market to rise at a $3 8 \%$ CAGR, 2023-2030e, reaching US $\$ 7536n$ , mainly driven by the strong cloud-related demand, including GPUs, AI ASICs, CPUs, and related peripherals. We also expect TSMC and its OSAT partners 加微信 LCD123321Cato continue expanding its front-end $2 / 3 n m$ -17  and CoWoS capacity.

Against this backdrop, we expect the global testing equipment market to grow strongly, reaching $\mathsf { U S S 7 1 . 5 b n }$ in 2026 $4 4 0 \%$ YoY) and $\cup \ S \$ 13.7$ bn in 2027 ( $+ 7 9 \%$ YoY), implying a ${ \sim } 3 5 \%$ CAGR over 2024–27e, with testing intensity remaining elevated at \~100bps. This growth is driven by shorter time-to-market requirements, HBM-driven demand for more advanced testing, and the increasing adoption of chiplet architectures, all of which expand testing across wafer, final, and system-level stages.

Within this ecosystem, high-end testing interfaces are seeing the strongest structural tailwinds: the probe card market is set to grow at $\sim 2 5 \%$ CAGR, led by MEMS and VPC adoption for high-pin-count AI chips, while the socket market is expected to grow at ${ \sim } 2 0 \%$ CAGR, supported by rising SLT/burn-in requirements and increasing package complexity. We see MPI benefiting from share gains in MEMS probe cards, while WinWay is well positioned to expand in VPC and 加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 advanced socket solutions (e.g., hypersockets), supported by deeper integration into TSMC and OSAT ecosystems and strong exposure to leading AI customers.

For the test and burn-in socket market, we project it will grow from $\mathsf { U S S 1 } , \mathsf { S 8 9 m n }$ in 2024 to $\cup 5 \$ 4,775 m$ in 2030e, implying a $20 \%$ CAGR, driven mainly by rising pin counts, longer testing times, and strong AI/HPC-related demand. Given WinWay's close relationships with major AI and HPC customers, including NVIDIA, AMD, and custom ASIC designers, we expect its market share to grow from $8 . 6 \%$ in 2024 to $20 \%$ by 2028e.

# How big is the AI TAM?

# 订阅研报

According to Gartner, the global semiconductor market is set for strong growth, supported by advances in AI processing, data center networking and power infrastructure, and a sharp increase in memory pricing. Gartner projects industry revenue to grow by 添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 63.9% in 2026 to US\$1.32trn, highlighting the semiconductor industry's increasingly important role in the AI value chain.

We expect AI semiconductors – the driving force behind this growth – to account for about $30 \%$ of total semiconductor sales in 2026, rising to nearly $45 \%$ by 2030. Hyperscaler investment in AI infrastructure build-outs remains robust, with spending projected to increase by more than $50 \%$ in 2026. This investment continues to drive demand for AI accelerators, including both GPU and custom non-GPU solutions.

The AI accelerator market represents the largest growth opportunity, with total revenue estimated at nearly $\mathsf { U S S 3 0 0 b n }$ across end-markets over the forecast period. Serverfocused accelerator sales are expected to increase by $7 1 \% ,$ reaching $\cup \mathsf { S$ 1 8 2 . 5 }$ bn in 2026, with a long-term CAGR of $30 \%$ driving the segment to approximately US\$400bn by 2030. While NVIDIA (covered by Joe Moore) should maintain its dominance in GPU-based AI acceleration, hyperscalers are actively advancing custom (non-GPU) AI accelerator strategies, supported by strategic partnerships with leading semiconductor vendors such as Broadcom, Marvell, and others.

Over the past two years, TSMC’s CoWoS capacity expansion and allocation have been key leading indicators for global AI GPU and AI ASIC revenue. While end customer demand – particularly from NVIDIA’s customers – represents the true underlying driver, we find that global cloud AI semi revenue forecasts are now converging with TSMC’s CoWoS implied chip shipments. The risks to AI semi foundry demand appear limited in 2026, as TSMC’s foundry capacity, especially 3nm, remains constrained. These supply limitations should help prevent oversupply and mitigate concerns around a potential “AI bubble” correction. We maintain our Attractive industry view on Greater China Semiconductors. However, from 2027 onwards, uncertainties may emerge on both the supply and demand sides:

• Supply side – fab cleanroom capacity: TSMC is approaching capacity limits in its existing fab cleanrooms in Taiwan, which is accelerating the need to ramp up overseas fab construction, particularly in the US. Demand side – power infrastructure: Even if TSMC can supply sufficient chips to meet customers’ strong AI demand, uncertainty remains whether power shortages in the US could be resolved to support large scale AI infrastructure deployment.

# TSMC and OSAT capex continues to increase

During TSMC's 4Q25 earnings call, the company guided full-year 2026 capex at $\cup \mathsf { S$ 52 } .$ 56bn, above our prior estimate of US\$49bn. Management also indicated that capex is likely to step up further in coming years, supported by very strong AI-related demand, and noted that tool costs continue to rise at leading-edge nodes.

Additionally, based on our recent conversations with TSMC’s equipment and chemical suppliers, we observe that its infrastructure build-out – particularly cleanroom additions – is proceeding more aggressively than we previously estimated. Between now and end-2027, TSMC plans to construct 12 front-end fabs, including advanced 3nm fabs in Arizona and Kumamoto, as well as four advanced packaging fabs. In light of these developments, we now forecast TSMC’s capex for 2027 to reach US\$65bn and 2028 capex at US\$70bn.

We also see a clear upward trend in OSATs capex. Total OSAT spend has increased from US\$4.7bn in 2023 following the post-Covid-19 correction, to $\cup \mathsf { S } \$ 8$ 15bn in 2026e. This increase is driven primarily by AI-related demand. Companies such as ASE are receiving increased outsourcing from TSMC, while testing houses such as KYEC are benefitting from longer test times for AI-related chips (e.g., GPUs). Other OSATs, including Amkor, JCET, and Powertech, are also developing more advanced packaging solutions.

# Global Testing Equipment Market

Our Morgan Stanley Global Semiconductors Research team published a deep-dive report on the global testing market (see Semiconductor Capital Equipment: Tester Market: Transforming into Growth Market on AI Boon; Advantest Our Top Pick (21 Aug 2024)), highlighting that the global testing market is becoming structurally stronger. We identify three key drivers underpinning this shift: 1) shorter time-to-market, which is increasing testing as a percentage of high-performance computing (HPC) product pricing, 2) DRAM evolution, particularly high-bandwidth memory (HBM), which is driving demand for new 添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 testers, and 3) the adoption of chiplet architectures, which is expanding test requirements across die-level, final, and system-level testing. Across these drivers, a common theme has emerged – testing is transitioning from a cost-minimization step where to a critical stage where customers place greater importance on improving yield and performance.

Our Morgan Stanley Global Semiconductors Research team currently forecasts the global testing TAM to reach $\mathsf { U S S 1 1 . 5 b n }$ $( + 4 0 \%$ Y/Y) in 2026 and $\cup S \$ 13.76 n$ $4 7 9 \%$ Y/Y) in 2027, implying that testing intensity remains elevated at \~100bps. This outlook reflects the structural shift of testing from a cost center to an increasingly value-add step in the semiconductor manufacturing process (for further details see the section, Global

# Testing Handler Market

Handlers are essential automation tools for picking and placing ICs during the final testing (FT) and system-level testing (SLT) stages. In the FT process, ICs are picked up from IC trays by handlers and positioned at the testing site for evaluation by testers such Advantest's V93000 made by or Teradyne's UltraFLEX. Each tester is paired with a handler, underscoring the handler's critical role in the testing value chain. Key handler suppliers include Hon Precision, Cohu, Chroma, Changchuan Technology, and JHT Semiconductor (JHT). Hon Precision holds over $70 \%$ market share in the AI/HPC handler segment and roughly $30 \%$ of the overall handler market.

Considering Hon Precision holds roughly $30 \%$ of the overall handler market, we expect handlers to account for $0 . 5 \%$ and $0 . 9 \%$ of global WFE market in 2024 and 2025, respectively. We expect the overall handler market to expand to around $1 . 7 \%$ of global WFE over time, driven primarily by strong AI demand, longer testing times, and rising testing requirements such as co-packaged optics (CPO). As the clear leader in the AI/HPC handler market with over $70 \%$ market share, we expect Hon Precision to further increase its overall handler market share from $30 \%$ in $2 0 2 4 - 2 5$ to $40 \%$ by 2027e. This market share expansion should support a revenue CAGR of $8 \%$ , 2024-27e, and $6 7 \%$ , 2025-28e.

# Probe Card Market – What is a Probe Card?

After wafer fabrication and entry into advanced packaging, and before dicing and CoWoS processing, each die must undergo testing. The testing cell comprises several components. Testers supplied by vendors such as Advantest and Teradyne generate test vectors and measure device responses. The prober handles the wafer and aligns it under tightly controlled environmental conditions. Positioned between the tester and the wafer is the probe card, which serves as the critical interface between the tester electronics and the microscopic pads on the wafer. the probe card carries electrical current, transmits highfrequency signals, and ensures mechanical compliance across thousands of contact points.

To simplify, a probe card is an electromechanical interface used to test chip functionality 添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 while the device remains on the wafer, prior to dicing and individual packaging. As advanced packaging has evolved, test interfaces have become inseparable from overall system performance. The probe card has therefore emerged as a critical enabler, ensuring that the dies entering advanced packaging are functional, reliable, and capable of withstanding the stresses of advanced assembly and operation.

This mechanical sensitivity requires low-force probing and highly compliant probe card systems, particularly when contacting small micro-bumps. In addition, positional variability of dies after singulation – often while still mounted on a film frame – demands sophisticated alignment capability and stable contact mechanics from the probe card to ensure consistent, multi-site testing. During wafer probing, the probe card makes physical contact with the chip’s pads or micro-bumps. Once proper contact is established, the probe card provides the electrical interface between the chip and the tester, enabling performance verification and defect identification. Because each chip design features distinctive pad layouts and electrical requirements, probe cards are custom-designed for the specific device being tested.

For high-power AI devices, transitional wafer-level probing is insufficient due to thermal limitation. High-power AI chips generate substantial heat during testing. Wafer-level thermal management relies largely on passive heat conduction through the silicon substrate, whereas die testing requires active thermal control to rapidly stabilize the temperature, prevent hot spot migration, and maintain consistent electrical behavior. In addition, physical factors such as electrical noise, contact resistance drift over time, and systematic wear or contamination of probe tips can introduce noise into measurement signals before digitization. As a result, the industry has begun deploying new solutions that incorporate active thermal control specifically for bare-die testing, allowing manufacturers to execute full, at-speed final-testing programs at the die level. The ability to guarantee known-good die (KGD) is now directly contingent upon the stability, precision, and physical compliance of the probing technology used in this critical screening step.

# How Is Probe Card Evolving?

The rapid scaling of AI and HPC workloads is driving a structural shift in probe card requirements, with high pin count emerging as a critical enabler of advanced device b¥mûR _®Oá       L C D 1 2 3 3 2 1 C a d       0 4 - 1 7   testing. As leading-edge processors adopt increasingly complex architectures – featuring chiplet designs, heterogeneous integration, and advanced packaging (e.g., 2.5D/3D) – the number of I/O connections per device continues to rise significantly. This trend requires probe cards that can support much higher pin densities while maintaining precise alignment and signal integrity. As a result, we expect sustained demand for vertical and MEMS probe technologies, which are better suited to accommodate ultra-high pin-count configurations than traditional cantilever solutions.

In parallel, the rising power requirements of AI accelerators and HPC chips are increasing the importance of high current-carrying capacity in probe card design. Next-generation GPUs and custom AI ASICs operate at higher current levels to support greater compute throughput and memory bandwidth, placing increased electrical and thermal stress on probe interfaces during wafer testing. Probe cards must therefore adopt materials and structures that minimize resistance and thermal buildup, ensuring stable electrical contact under high-load conditions. This trend is likely to accelerate the adoption of advanced probe materials and enhanced thermal-management solutions, as reliability and yield become increasingly sensitive to current-induced degradation during testing.

Finally, the need for high-speed signal transmission is becoming a defining feature of probe card evolution, driven by the growing adoption of HBM, chip-to-chip interconnects, and high-frequency SerDes interfaces in AI and HPC devices. Testing these components requires probe cards that can support multi-gigahertz signal frequencies with minimal signal loss, crosstalk, and impedance mismatch. As a result, probe card suppliers are investing in advanced design architectures and materials to enable high-frequency performance, including improved shielding, shorter signal paths, and tighter impedance and thermal challenges will be best positioned to benefit from the next phase of AI-driven semiconductor scaling.

# #2: Larger Package Sizes Are Emerging as a Key Industry Trend

As semiconductor scaling approaches physical limits below 2nm, performance improvements are increasingly driven by advanced packaging and chiplet architectures (2.5D/3D). These approaches no only enable integration of multiple 加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 dies – such as GPUs and HBM – within a single package, but also result in larger, more complex packages with significantly higher interconnect density.

These trends are materially increasing testing intensity, as a greater number of dies require validation and advanced packages demand more stringent testing, including burn-in, FT, and growing adoption of SLT). As a result, test times are rising, led by NVIDIA, with similar trends expected to extend across AMD and ASIC players.

As a result, the testing ecosystem is a key beneficiary, including testing equipment vendors (ATE, handlers) and consumables suppliers such as probe cards and socket vendors. This benefit is driven by higher pin counts, greater customization, and more stringent durability requirements. In addition, HBM further amplifies demand, as test times are typically 5-10x longer than those for traditional DRAM. Overall, testing is becoming a critical bottleneck and a structural growth driver in the semiconductor value chain.

# 订阅研报

添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 As transistor dimensions continue to shrink below 2nm, wafer manufacturing is approaching fundamental physical limits. At the same time, growing demand for semiconductor circuit scale and functionality has driven greater integration of semiconductor dies and more advanced package substrate technologies. As higher levels of integration and leading-edge process advances continue to weigh on cost efficiency, the adoption of chiplet architectures – combining multiple dies on a single package substrate – has become essential to achieving further gains in overall semiconductor performance

Behind this trend, once multiple functions are integrated into a single semiconductor die, die size increases, driving higher manufacturing complexity and costs. As a result, industry focus has shifted toward methods that mount multiple semiconductor elements on a single package substrate to enhance overall semiconductor performance, including the following approaches:

# 订阅研报

• 3D, where semiconductor dies are stacked vertically, and   
• 2.5D, where semiconductor elements are connected through silicon or organic   
加 微信 interposers on the package substrate.

To integrate more transistors into a single chip and deliver higher performance, foundries, integrated device manufacturers (IDMs), and outsourced semiconductor assembly and test (OSAT) providers are actively developing 2.5D and 3D packaging technologies. These include TSMC’s CoWoS and SoIC, as well as emerging solutions like chip-on-panel-onsubstrate (CoPoS) and fan-out panel-level packaging (FOPLP). By vertically stacking dies on interposers and substrates, advanced packaging enables a continuous increases in effective transistor density, supporting ongoing performance gains and extending the

practical limits of Moore’s Law.

Larger substrate sizes and higher layer counts are becoming increasingly evident, reflecting the need for greater electrical interconnect density to support data transfer, control signals, and power delivery between the AI accelerator dies and HBM memory stacks. This shift toward larger, more complex substrates is well established and shows no signs of reversing in the foreseeable future.

The sharp increase in the number of dies integrated within a single chip has materially raised testing demand, as each die must be tested at least once prior to packaging to 添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 prevent a single defective die compromising the entire device. At the same time, advanced packaging further intensifies testing requirement. Densely stacked dies – particularly for AI and HPC applications – generate substantial heat and operate under higher power and voltage conditions, requiring more sophisticated and precise testing methodologies to ensure reliability and performance. We believe testing equipment and consumables suppliers like probe card and socket vendors are well positioned to benefit from the advanced packaging trend, supported by both higher volumes – driven by a increased die counts – and higher ASPs, reflecting the need for high-end testing interfaces capable of withstanding elevated power, voltage, and thermal stress.

As package sizes increase, testing times are also rising, which in turn drives higher demand for testing equipment. NVIDIA’s FT time should continue lengthening with the introduction of new platforms such as Rubin in 2026 and Rubin Ultra in 2027, leading to incremental demand for testing capacity. AMD and other ASIC vendors, including Google's TPU, are also gradually converging toward NVIDIA’s path of stringent testing 添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 requirements as their AI accelerator designs grow in scale and complexity. NVIDIA has set the industry benchmark with highly sophisticated test design procedures, adopting some of the most comprehensive test programs. In addition to two rounds of FT, its AI accelerators also undergo burn-in and SLT test insertions, further elevating overall testing intensity.

Meanwhile, NVIDIA's FT time has continued to lengthen as test programs become more complex. We estimate FT time more than doubled from Hopper to Blackwell, and could rise another $3 0 { - } 5 0 \%$ with the introduction of the Rubin platform in 2026. Rubin Ultra in 2027 could represent another meaningful step-change in FT time, as the platform is expected to double the number of GPUs and HBM stacks. NVIDIA's strong AI GPU unit demand, combined with rich FT content, has driven meaningful business upside for testing suppliers over 2023-24, including Advantest (FT tools), KYEC (FT service provider), and Hon Precision (handler). We view this growth as structural and, in our view, should be sustainable over the coming years.

However, we see substantial upside over the next few years, driven by the shift toward larger package sizes, increased GPU and HBM counts, and more complex die and package architectures. From 2026E onward, AMD and ASIC vendors could materially increase demand for testing capacity. For example, in the cloud ASIC segment, MediaTek's TPU v7e ramping from late 2026e may adopt more comprehensive testing programs – including burn-in and two rounds of FT – and we expect other ASIC programs to follow a similar path.

# What are the steps within the testing market?

After the fabrication process, a single semiconductor die undergoes a series of rigorous tests to verify functionality, reliability, and performance before, during, and after it is packaged into a final chip alongside other dies. The process begins with chip probing (CP), 添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 where individual dies on the wafer are tested for basic electrical functionality using probe cards. This step identifies defective units prior to packaging. Defective dies are screened out during the wafer sort, while the remaining KGD dies proceed to the packaging stage. Once the dies are assembled into a packaged chip, the device undergoes burn-in testing, which applies elevated temperatures and voltages to accelerate early-life failures and ensure long-term reliability. This is followed by FT, which provides a comprehensive assessment of the packaged chip’s performance under operating conditions, including parameters such as speed, power, and signal integrity. For advanced applications, SLT integrates the chip into a simulated or actual system environment to validate real-world performance, compatibility, and robustness. This multi-stage testing pipeline is essential to meeting the increasingly stringent quality requirements of modern semiconductor applications, as chip complexity and unit value continue to rise.

# Chipslets increase test processes

Chiplets bring testing challenges and opportunities. Compared to monolithic devices, 添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 chiplet architectures require wafer-level testing of individual sub-components as well as partial-assembly testing prior to final testing to protect overall yield. As a result, test customers face two key trade-offs: 1) Test cost: Customers may choose to implement wafer testing, partial-assembly testing, and final testing, which increases total test cost relative to monolithic devices, and 2) Scrap cost: Relative to monolithic devices, scrap costs rise non-linearly as products move closer to final assembly, forcing customers to decide at which stages to insert additional testing steps.

![](images/c436a5ef516ddf58c0bb6e3b7cc551fd7373fb0cf7d9a51767aafabb656cbb13.jpg)

# 订阅研报添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17

Chiplets to drive SLT. SLTs are conducted by emulating the end-user environment. Historically, SLT has served a complementary role in semiconductor testing, applied to limited sample sizes to validate system performance and functionality of final products. However, as semiconductors have become more more complex – with higher operating speeds and the adoption of advanced high-density multi-die packages – instances have increased where fully assembled fail to meet specifications despite passing standalone tests. In response, semiconductor manufacturers are increasingly adopting SLT more broadly, using it alongside traditional standalone device testing to validate performance under real-world system conditions.

![](images/3f31450b79c2e43e299d202b8cd76ed509f938caf8f4b35b313e46dab88a7ab7.jpg)  
Exhibit 57: Role of system level testing   
Source: Teradyne.

SLT basic design: To perform SLT, an IC socket is mounted onto a dedicated test board that is specifically designed for the device under test. In addition to IC sockets, these test boards typically integrate power management ICs, RAM, storage, SD cards, USB flash, and relevant communications interfaces. They also incorporate thermal control capabilities, enabling testing under temperature conditions that closely replicate real-world operating conditions.

Chip makers seem tolerant of higher test costs resulting from SLT: Semiconductor manufacturers historically aimed to limit testing costs to ${ \sim } 3 \%$ of total manufacturing costs and were cautious about the additional costs associated with adopting SLT. However, broader adoption of SLT has enabled more efficient and effective testing workflows, including improved utilization of automated test equipment (ATE). At the same time, lower product return rate have strengthened companies’ brand reputation, leading manufacturers to accept higher testing costs associated with SLT. As SLT introduces additional test insertions beyond FT, we believe tester manufacturers can capture incremental earnings growth by positioning SLT as a core business opportunity rather than a supplementary testing step.

# Memory: HBM

Among advanced packaging elements, HBM is the most disruptive for probe cards.

Each HBM stack contains 8-16 dies vertically connected by Through-Silicon Via technology (TSVs), which interface with GPUs or AI ASICs via ultra-wide buses of $1 , 0 2 4 - 2 , 0 4 8$ bits with pitch scaling from 55um/45um today toward 36um for HBM4. More importantly, probe cards are not commodity products. Each probe card is custom-designed for a specific chip, reflecting unique pad layouts, electrical requirements, and performance specifications.

Engagement includes:

1. Jointly design-in with chipmakers and ATE vendors.   
2. Long qualification cycles, usually 12-18 months   
3. Volume production that is synchronized with wafer ramp-up

4. Recurring maintenance, refurbishment, and needle replacement.

Going forward, we expect continued increases in pin count, current-handling requirements, and testing coverage for chiplet-based designs and stacked memory architectures. The evolution of advanced packaging has elevated wafer probing from a routine screening step to the first line of defense again costly downstream yield losses, reinforcing its critical role in the semiconductor manufacturing flow.

HBM testing intensity extremely high. Bit growth has been the primary driver of conventional memory testing. However, the architectural characteristics of HBM make it 添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 far more test intensive. HBM requires additional testing due to die stacking, higher memory density, and more demanding performance requirements. While the same memory testers are used for both initial DRAM wafer testing and post stacking tests, HBM imposes more stringent test conditions, particularly around speed and functional coverage. Given the high end product value of HBM, memory manufacturers are incentivized to prioritize defect minimization over tester cost optimization. We estimate that HBM DRAM testing times are $5 \mathrm { - } 7 0 \times$ longer than those for smartphone DRAM. We also estimate that adding $2 0 0 \boldsymbol { \mathrm { k } }$ WPM of HBM capacity would require test capacity equivalent to the current global DRAM manufacturing base of \~1,200k WPM, implying a doubling of the installed DRAM tester fleet ( Exhibit 58 ).

New generations of HBM to bring more test challenges. New generations of HBM feature higher stack counts, finer die geometries, fast interface speeds, and increased power requirements. Together, these factors materially raise testing intensity through longer test time and more stringent test requirements. In addition, compared with the 添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 traditional DDR cycle, HBM follows a much faster technology cadence, which in some cases necessitates incremental tester purchases to keep pace with rapid product transitions.

# #3: New Evolution for Testing Equipment and Components: CPO, Micro-channel Lid

Co-packaged optics (CPO), micro-channel lid (MCL) cooling, and HBM are driving a new wave of semiconductor testing complexity, reinforcing testing as a critical bottleneck in the value chain.

微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 • CPO introduces multi-stage testing, typically involving one to four test insertions, to ensure known-good optical engines. From 2027 onward, the adoption of SLT is expected to further drive demand for test equipment and consumables, including probe cards and sockets.

• MCL cooling adds new testing requirements, such as leakage and structural integrity. These requirements create opportunities for handlers and thermal-testing solutions, with volume ramp-up expected from late 2026.

• HBM materially increases testing intensity, with test times typically 5-10x longer than those for conventional DRAM, driven by die stacking, higher bandwidth, and elevated power requirements. This trend directly benefits probe card suppliers and drives demand for additional testing capacity.

Overall, these developments are structurally increasing the number of test steps, test duration, and overall testing complexity, supporting sustained growth for 加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-1testing equipment and consumables vendors.

# Co-packaged optics

In co-packaged optics (CPO), “insertion testing” typically refers to the defined test insertion points throughout the manufacturing flow, specifying where and how opticalrelated testing is conducted from wafer level through final system integration. The primary objective of these insertion tests is to identify the known good optical engines (KGOE) prior to integration with SoC/ASIC. In practice, current CPO manufacturing flows typically include three to four test insertion stages. The associated measurements can be broadly grouped into three domains: photonic integrated circuit (PIC) signals, optical output signals from the optical engine, and system level electrical and optical signals. However, as CPO technology remains at an early stage of development, TSMC and its OSAT partners are still in the process of defining and standardizing the full insertion test flow, while concurrently qualifying suitable testing equipment and ecosystem partners.

Insertion 1 typically refers to PIC (Photonic IC) Wafer-Level Test. The objective is to screen core photonic components such as modulators, photodiodes, and power monitors before downstream processing. Typical test items include optical power, insertion loss, dark current, and basic DC electrical/optical characteristics. This early screening step plays a critical role in removing defective dies upfront, thereby protecting yield and reducing losses in subsequent manufacturing stages.

Insertion 2 typically takes place after the COUPE/SoIC stage, when electronic IC (EIC) dies are bonded onto the PIC wafer, while testing is still conducted at the wafer level. This

stage focuses on electro-optical (EO/OE) performance and the interaction between photonic and electronic components. Key measurements include modulation efficiency, receiver sensitivity, EO/OE conversion characteristics, and overall link performance. The objective is to verify that the integrated electrical and optical subsystems operate correctly and cohesively before proceeding to further assembly steps.

Insertion 3 is performed after the optical engines are diced individually from the PIC wafers and is typically conducted at the OSAT stage. Before co-packaging with the ASIC, each optical engine undergoes testing to quality as a KGOE. This stage includes high-speed (at-speed) testing such as bit-error-rate (BER), eye-diagram, and jitter analysis, as well as assessments of power consumption, thermal behavior, and wavelength stability. This represents one of the most critical test insertion points, as it shifts defect detection earlier in the manufacturing flow and materially reduces downstream integration risk.

Insertion 4 occurs after the optical engine is co-packaged with the ASIC, at which point a final test is performed on the fully packaged device using both electrical and optical interfaces. Typical test coverage includes electrical functionality, optical link integrity, transmit/receive channel verification, multi-channel consistency, and performance under thermal conditions. This stage closely resembles traditional ATE-based final testing, but with added optical complexity. The final insertion is SLT, which evaluates the device under mission-mode, real-world operating conditions. SLT typically involves running realistic traffic patterns, conducting long duration stress tests, monitoring error rates, and assessing thermal electrical optical interactions. The objective is to validate end system performance, stability, and reliability under actual use conditions.

阅研报添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 Testing by NVIDIA's Spectrum and Quantum platforms that entered mass production in 2025 and 2026, respectively, stops at Insertion 3. At this stage, OSAT will primarily conduct electrical testing on the packaged devices, while the end-customer will rely on earlier insertion steps to identify known-good PIC dies. Insertion $4$ is expected to be introduced in 2027e, coinciding with the next generation of Quantum for scale-up deployment in the Rubin Ultra platform.

# 添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17Micro-channel Lid

A micro-channel lid (MCL) is an advanced thermal lid in which a network of micro-scale channels is embedded within a metal cap, allowing coolant to circulate actively through the lid. Our Morgan Stanley Greater China Technology Hardware Research team believes MCL could become a viable thermal design option for next generation chip platforms, as chip TDPs continue to rise and exceed the capabilities of existing cooling solutions. That said, we expect MCL adoption to be gradual, as challenges remain around manufacturing yield, leakage detection, and long term reliability qualification.

An MCL reduces warpage by addressing both the thermal and mechanical drivers of deformation.

• By significantly lowering the peak die temperature and minimizing lateral temperature gradients, MCL reduces thermally induced strain arising from coefficient of thermal expansion (CTE) mismatch among the die, package, and   
加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17substrate.   
• The lid itself acts as a stiff structural layer, increasing the bending rigidity of the die-lid stack and shifting the neutral axis closer to the die, thereby reducing curvature under thermal loading.

Together, the combination of more uniform cooling, reduced thermal excursions, and added stiffness makes MCLs particularly effective at mitigating both static and transient warpage in large, high-power packages.

Hon Precision currently offers a range of cold-plate solutions tailored to individual customers, as IC package size and design vary with power consumption requirements. Cold plates, used in conjunction with ATC, help maintain target temperatures during testing. In addition to bundled handler, ATC, and cold-plate solutions, some customers also procure cold plates on a stand-alone basis as older-generation ICs are phased out and new components are introduced. As a result, cold plates have become an increasingly important growth segment for Hon Precision.

Hon Precision is also at the forefront of supporting emerging cooling technologies,   
including MCL. Hon Precision is working closely with end-customers such as NVIDIA, as   
well as MCL ecosystem partners, to develop handlers capable of validating MCL 332 ad   
structures during the FT process.

Semiconductor testing with MCL is highly complex. Final designs can incorporate multiple liquid inlets and outlets feeding micrometer-sized channels. Hon Precision's handlers must therefore validate MCL quality throughout the FT process. A key objective is to ensure that the MCL structure remains intact during testing, particularly as the handler applies up to $3 0 0 \mathsf { k g }$ of pressure when pressing the MCL-packaged IC packaged onto the test socket. Prior to FT, the handler must inspect the MCL surface to confirm structural integrity. During FT, the handler is required to remove inlet and outlet caps, introduce coolant, and verify the absence of leakage. After testing, the handler must clean the inlets and outlets and conduct a final inspection to ensure that no damage has occurred to the MCL. While the broader entire supply chain continues to develop and qualify MCL technology, we expect trial or small-volume production to begin around 2027, coinciding with the Rubin Ultra generation. We believe MCL adoption has the potential to raise entry barriers for handler turnkey solutions, given the added technical and process complexity.

Our supply-chain checks indicate that significant volumes of MCLs were shipped to endcustomers at the end of March for qualification. Assuming the testing process proceeds smoothly, we expect volume production of Hon Precision's MCL handlers to begin in 4Q26.

# Memory: HBM

HBM test intensity extremely high. Bit growth has historically driven conventional memory testing, but HBM’s architecture makes it far more test intensive. HBM requires additional testing due to die stacking, higher memory density, and more demanding performance requirements. While the same memory testers support both initial DRAM wafer testing and post stacking testing, HBM imposes significantly more stringent test conditions, particularly in terms of speed and functional coverage. Given the high end product value of HBM, memory manufacturers prioritize defect minimization over rationalizing tester spending. We estimate that HBM DRAM testing times are $5 \mathrm { - } 7 0 \times$ longer than those for smartphone DRAM. We also estimate that adding 200k wafers per month (WPM) of HBM capacity would require test capacity equivalent to the current global DRAM manufacturing base of \~1,200k WPM, effectively doubling the installed DRAM tester fleet (see Exhibit 75 ).

New generations of HBM to bring more test challenges. New generations of HBM feature higher stack counts, continued die miniaturization, faster interface speeds, and increased power requirements. These factors collectively drive higher testing intensity through longer test times and more stringent test coverage. In addition, compared with the traditional DDR cycle, HBM follows a much shorter technology cadence, which in certain cases necessitates incremental tester purchases to support rapid product transitions.

Among advanced packaging technologies, HBM is the most disruptive for probe card requirements. Each HBM stack comprises 8–16 dies vertically interconnected through TSVs, which connect to GPUs or AI ASICs via ultra wide interfaces of 1,024–2,048 bits, with pitch scaling from ${ \sim } 5 5 \mu \mathrm { m } / 4 5 \mu \mathrm { m }$ today toward ${ \sim } 3 6 \mu \mathrm { m }$ for HBM4. Importantly, probe cards are not commodity products—each card is custom designed for a specific chip, reflecting unique pad layouts, electrical requirements, and performance specifications.

Going forward, we expect continued increases in pin counts, current handling requirements, and test coverage for chiplet based designs and stacked memory architectures. The evolution of advanced packaging has repositioned wafer probing as the first line of defense against costly downstream yield losses.

Our supply-chain checks also point to emerging opportunities for MPI in the HBM and memory probe card segment, which is currently dominated by MJC.

# Global Implications

# The Japan semiconductor market and the US semiconductor production equipment market (covered by Suzune Tamura and Shane Brett)

Test intensity to remain high in 2026. Test intensity (i.e., test TAM as a percentage of semiconductor sales) reached 105bps in 2025, surpassing 100bps for the first time in our 添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 model (since 2010) and exceeding the prior peak of 97/98bps in 2020/21. For SoC test specifically, intensity reached 115bps in 2025 versus a prior peak of 96/108bps in 2020/21. In a February report, we raised our test TAM forecast to $\cup 5 \$ 123,76 n$ $( + 1 9 \%$ y/y) in $2 0 2 6 / 2 7 ,$ implying test intensity remains elevated, at \~100bps. This reflects the structural evolution of test from a cost center to, increasingly, a value-add step in the semiconductor manufacturing process.

2026 is set to mark the full-scale ramp-up of test demand for AI ASICs. As of 2025, AI ASIC-related sales accounted for less than $10 \%$ of Advantest’s SoC tester revenue; however, we expect this share to rise to a meaningful level in 2026, reaching the low teens of the percent range. In the December quarter results, tester sales exceeded company expectations, with expanding demand for AI ASICs being one of the key drivers. 添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 Demand for testers for AI GPUs also remains robust. While we expect tester sales for GPUs to continue growing in 2026, we anticipate that tester sales for AI ASICs will expand at a faster pace than those for GPUs.

Memory testers are also expected to deliver strong growth. While Advantest has highlighted plans to expand its SoC tester production capacity – from 3,000 units annually at the beginning of 2025 to 5,000 units by the end of 2026, and further to 7,500 units within the following few years – the company has indicated that it will scale memory tester supply capacity at a similar pace. Although we project that memory tester sales will decelerate in $F 3 / 2 6$ (from ${ > } 8 0 \%$ growth YoY in $\mathsf { F 3 } / 2 5$ to $+ 9 \%$ in $\textsf { F 3 } / 2 6 \mathsf { E } )$ , we expect a

return to a strong growth trajectory from F3/27 onward.

Die-level test (DLT) is emerging as a potential additional test insertion, especially tied to advanced packaging and chiplet flows. DLT is positioned between wafer sort and final test, after dicing. While wafer level testing has constraints in test conditions, die level testing enables higher power delivery, tighter thermal control, and longer stress conditions that better emulate in use operation, which wafer level testing cannot achieve. Tokyo Electron says die level probers could account for ${ \sim } 1 0 { - } 1 5 \%$ of the overall prober market in five years, while it expects the prober market total will be expanding at a $1 5 \%$ sales CAGR. This kind of new test insertion will also drive test market growth in the longer term. 信

# Strong AI demand triggers high OSAT capex

In our Back-end Observations: ASE and KYEC AI semi revenue forecasts, we further break down the AI demand for major packaging/testing players, ASE and KYEC, leading to strong capex, as well as the need for probe card and handler testing.

Our bottom-up analysis suggests that ASE's LEAP revenue is likely to reach $\cup 5 \$ 3.56 n$ in 2026 and $\mathsf { U S S 5 5 . 5 b n }$ in 2027, given strong demand from both oS (on substrate) outsourcing and CPU CoWoS needs, slightly higher than company guidance of $\cup S S 3 . 2 6 \mathsf { n }$ . The demand is mostly from oS outsourcing from TSMC. While ASE remains the major oS supplier to NVIDIA GPU, our checks also indicate that the majority of Google TPU and 添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 AMD MI400 series is likely to be outsourced to ASE, given TSMC's clean-room limitations. The dollar content per chip for oS at ASE could range from $\cup \mathsf { S$ 100 }$ to $\cup \mathsf { S$ 200 }$ in 2026, depending on the material cost, chip size, and margin of each product. Our current assumption of the oS outsourcing percentage from TSMC of major products is – NVIDIA almost $100 \%$ , TPU $70 . 9 0 \%$ , MI400 $80 \%$ .

Meanwhile, we expect that AMD's Venice CPU could go for full CoWoS-like packaging at ASE Group (both ASE and SPIL). The initial allocation could be more at SPIL given SPIL's better production capability. Then, the allocation could reach 50:50 between SPIL and ASE. By assuming $\mathsf { U S$ 8 k }$ per CoWoS wafer cost at ASE and that the chip per CoWoS wafer could be around 25-26 chips for Venice CPU, we expect the dollar content per Venice CPU to be slightly above $\cup \mathsf { S$ 300 }$ .

Besides the major products mentioned above, we also expect Vera CPU and other AI ASIC chips (Microsoft Maia, Meta MTIA, etc.) to go through either oS or full CoWoS-like process at ASE, but the volume is relatively small.

# KYEC also benefits from strong GPU/TPU/CPU demand

Initially, our assumption of MediaTek's TPU FT time was around 450 seconds; however, our recent checks suggest that the FT time could start from 500-600 seconds, around $20 \%$ higher than our assumption at the midpoint. Furthermore, the burn-in time of four hours, similar to its major customer's B-series XPU, is also slightly higher than our expectation of three hours. We therefore see business upside for KYEC because it is likely to handle the testing service for the majority of MediaTek's TPU; the benefit could be mostly in 2027 and 2028.

In our earlier GUC report, we mentioned that the raised demand forecast is not just for Google TPU, but CPU as well. We therefore raised our expectation of Google CPU shipments from 1mn to $\mathsf { 1 . 5 m n }$ this year. Our recent checks suggest that Google is likely to attach $100 \%$ of its own CPU in the server rack with MediaTek TPU and future generations. Our checks also indicate that this Google CPU requests burn-in and SLT (system-level testing), which we believe could generate $1 - 2 \%$ of KYEC 2026 revenue. To sum up, we now 添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 forecast that Google-related demand could account for ${ \sim } 1 0 \%$ of KYEC's 2026 total revenue, including TPU, CPU, FT, and part of CP (vs $1 - 2 \%$ of total revenue in 2025).

# 订阅研报

Overall, AI-related offerings may still contribute $2 5 - 3 0 \%$ to KYEC's revenue in 2025 and ${ > } 3 5 \%$ in 2026 (including TPU and chip probing). In addition, from our latest checks, the final test time of B-series XPU from its largest customers is recovering to the 1,000s, suggesting smooth capacity ramp-up at KYEC and the need for maintaining chip performance/yield through testing, which has been a major investor debate. Overall revenue from the largest customer should be more than $40 \%$ of KYEC's total revenue in

2026, we estimate.

After strong 2025 capex of NT\$37bn, KYEC announced even higher capex of NT\$50bn for 2026, suggesting strong demand and long-term commitment from its customers. We believe the strong AI demand could offset weaker consumer and automotive demand. Our estimate for 2026 capex breaks down as follows:

• Testers $2 0 { - } 3 0 \%$   
• Probers/handlers 5-15%   
• Burn-in 5-15%   
加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 • Facility/clean room 50%   
• Others $5 \%$

# Greater China Hardware (covered by Derrick Yang)

Chroma (2360.TW): Multiple Drivers from the AI-related Testing and Metrology Market

Dominant in GPU SLT, and stepping into FT handlers

Chroma has been the dominant supplier of SLT tools in the HPC space. It has been the sole supplier to NVIDIA's GPUs and also secured orders from AMD's MI450 series and other ASIC accelerator projects, as well. Moreover, the autonomous driving application has also been driving its revenue growth since last year.

Besides the expanding demand for GPU and accelerators in AI deployment, Chroma's SLT business should also benefit from the ever-increasing power density from those advanced chips as the test equipment specs will need to be upgraded too. We estimate that the SLT business will account for $20 \%$ of Chorma's total revenue in 2026.

添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 In the HPC testing market, Chroma used to focus more on SLT, but it has decided to leverage its existing expertise and customer relationships to penetrate the final test handler market, which we believe represents an even bigger market opportunity than SLT. Chroma is now qualifying its equipment with its HPC customers and is likely to have some revenue contribution from 2027. This is currently not in our model yet, so any progress here could be accretive to our revenue and earnings estimates.

![](images/ba36ae667edc31fa58f4b4b55669fd7e3f9f37b5b6e8f1d74006f3137910325c.jpg)  
Exhibit 82: SLT and FT equipment offerings from Chroma   
Source: Chroma.

# Fast-growing metrology business amid advanced packaging capacity expansions

In 2025, Chroma started to supply metrology equipment that is used in the probe mark, RDL and TSV structures in the advanced packaging process, with its customer base including both foundry and OSAT players.

Chroma's metrology equipment employs white light interferometry technology. The basic 添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 working principle is to have white light emitted on the reference mirror and the sample, which are placed at the same distance from the light source. The reflected beams from the reference mirror and sample surface are then received by a light sensor.

Interference occurs when two light beams collide, which could cause them to either strengthen or weaken, depending on the phase of the two light beams. Thus, by measuring the intensity of the combined light beams, the distance between the light sensor and multiple points on the target surface can be calculated through an algorithm. This can then be used to measure the dimensions of the structure or the alignment of

layers.

This business should continue to grow along with the capacity expansions for different advanced packaging architectures, e.g., CoWoS, wafer-level multi-chip module (WMCM), CoPoS. We estimate that metrology will account for $8 \%$ of Chroma's total revenue in 2026.

# Emerging contribution from CPO testing equipment

For CPO testing equipment, Chroma can leverage its accumulated experience and technology in electrical IC and photonics IC testing. It has already been qualified for its offering in the electrical/optical testing for optical engines and the CPO switch ASIC. Management indicates that it has already received orders, which add up to more than those for its metrology tools. It also has the testing equipment for the PIC wafer ready, and it is engaging with potential customers for future adoption.

We estimate that photonics testing equipment will account for $9 \%$ of its total revenue in 2026, and the contribution could be higher if CPO continues to penetrate into more

datacenter interconnects.

# Hon Precision (7769.TW): Key Beneficiary of the Structural Trend Toward Longer Testing Times; Initiate at OW

Hon Precision is a global leader in semiconductor test handler solutions, offering turnkey test design services for its customers. We view it as the global leader in AI/HPC final test handlers ${ \sim } 7 0 \%$ global market share, 2025), and as well positioned to 订阅研报添加微信benefit from structurally rising test intensity driven by larger, L more complex, and higher-power AI chips, which extend test times and increase demand per unit. Its patent-protected active thermal control (ATC) technology provides a key competitive edge as thermal requirements escalate (with AI chip TDP trending toward >4kW), supporting ongoing system upgrades, higher content, and $1 5 \mathrm { - } 2 0 \%$ ASP growth on an annual basis, we estimate. In addition, emerging test insertions, such as system-level test (SLT), alongside WMCM and CPO, offer incremental growth optionality as adoption broadens across AI players, with Hon Precision investing in multi-site SLT and automation to capture future share gains.

Earnings: Our EPS estimates for 2026-27 are more bullish than consensus. We think revenue momentum will remain strong during this period, thanks to Hon Precision's position as a global leader in semiconductor automated test handler solutions.

Valuation: We derive our price target from a residual income model, as we believe this can best capture the long-term value. Our price target implies 43x 2026e P/E, which we think is justified given the $6 \%$ revenue CAGR that we project for 2025-2028e.

Key downside risks include: 1) slower-than-expected AI demand, 2) stronger-than-expected market share gain from peers, and 3) slower-than-expected CPO development.

# NT\$5,000.00PRICE TARGET

We derive our price target from a residual income model, as we believe this can best capture the long-term value. We assume a $9 . 8 \%$ cost of equity (risk-free rate $2 . 0 \%$ and risk premium $6 . 0 \%$ , a payout ratio of $6 2 \%$ a medium-term growth rate of $1 0 \% ,$ and a terminal growth rate of $3 . 0 \%$ . Our price target implies $4 3 \times / 2 6 \times / 1 6 \times 2 0 2 6 \mathrm { e } / 2 0 2 7 \mathrm { e } / 2 0 2 8 \mathrm { e } \mathsf { P } / \mathsf { E } .$

# OVERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ We rate the shares Overweight relative to our coverage because it is the clear leader in ${mathsf { A l / H P C } }$ test handlers ${ \sim } 7 0 \%$ market share), with direct leverage to structurally rising test intensity, longer test times, and is 04-17 structurally positioned to bene fit from rising test intensity per chip.

▪ Hon’s patent-protected ATC technology is a key differentiator as thermal challenges intensify. We estimate that Hon is positioned to drive $1 5 - 2 0 \% +$ ASP growth through spec upgrades, enabling margin expansion alongside revenue growth.

▪ New test stages are expanding the TAM, including CPO, microchannel lid, and WMCM, providing incremental revenue streams and long-term share expansion opportunities.

# 41x 2027e EPS

We assume: 1) $100 \%$ revenue CAGR, 2025- 28e, on stronger-than-expected adoption of tri-temp ATC+handler with robust market share gain momentum in dual-temp ATC+handler and more market share gains; 2) faster-than-expected CPO introduction; 3) gross margin improves to over $70 \%$ thanks to scale effects and the company's technology competitiveness; and $4$ ) $> 1 5 0 \%$ earnings CAGR, 2025-28e.

# 26x 2027e EPS

The introduction of next-gen AI GPU (including NVIDIA, AMD and MediaTek) should drive a signi cant increase in the content value for testing, requiring longer testing time and more testing procedures needed, such as adding SLT into its process. Combined with the multiplier effect emerging in CPO market, we expect these drivers to help Hon Precision’s 2025-28e revenue CAGR reach $6 \%$ . We expect to see operating leverage, with the earnings CAGR at $6 5 \%$ , 2025-28e.

# 13x 2027e EPS

We assume: 1) $20 \%$ revenue CAGR, 2025- 28e, on slower-than-expected adoption of tri-temp ATC+handler with robust market share gain momentum in dual-temp ${ \bar { \mathsf { A T C } } } +$ Handler and no project wins in CPO development; 2) slower-than-expected CPO introduction; 3) customer bargaining power causes gross margin to fall below $4 0 \% \cdot$ and 4) $30 \%$ earnings CAGR, 2025-28e.

# Hon Precision: Investment Positives and Concerns

Investment positives

# Structural AI/HPC Test Intensity Drives Sustained Handler Demand: Hon

Precision is the global leader in AI/HPC final test handlers, with $\sim 7 0 \%$ market share (by revenue in 2025), and it is structurally positioned to benefit from rising test intensity driven by AI semiconductor scaling. As AI accelerators become 微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 larger, more complex, and increasingly power-dense, testing requirements are expanding both in duration and complexity. This results in longer test times per unit and higher handler utilization, even before factoring in continued growth in AI chip volumes across GPUs, TPUs, and custom ASICs. We believe this dynamic creates a durable, structural demand tailwind for Hon Precision, allowing it to capture growth from both increasing unit shipments and rising test content per chip.

# 2. Active Thermal Control (ATC) Leadership Enables Content Expansion and ASP

Upside: Hon Precision’s patent-protected ATC technology underpins its   
competitive positioning and supports sustained content and pricing expansion. With AI chip thermal design power rapidly increasing – from ${ \sim } 1 . 2 { - } 1 . 5 { \sf k W }$ in currentgeneration Blackwell GPUs, to potentially over 4kW in future architectures – thermal management during testing is becoming significantly more challenging. This is driving an industry shift from traditional air-cooled systems to more advanced liquid and refrigerant-based cooling solutions. As a result, handler 微信 C 123321Ca   
systems are becoming more complex, increasing both bill-of-materials content and technological barriers to entry. We estimate Hon Precision can achieve at least 15- $20 \%$ ASP uplift driven by ongoing specification upgrades, supporting both revenue growth and margin expansion.

# 3. Emerging Test Insertions (SLT, WMCM, CPO) Unlock New Growth Potential:

Beyond core final test, emerging test insertions, such as system-level test (SLT), along with developments in WMCM and co-packaged optics (CPO), represent incremental growth opportunities that could expand Hon Precision’s addressable market over time. SLT is gaining traction as hyperscalers and chipmakers seek to validate real-world system performance across full-stack environments, including CPU, memory, networking, and power delivery. While Hon Precision currently has exposure to SLT through AMD and automotive customers, broader adoption across AI players – including potential ramp from ASIC vendors such as Google in 2026 – could provide further upside. Although NVIDIA’s SLT supply remains 微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 dominated by Chroma in the near term, Hon Precision is investing in enhanced multi-site SLT and automation capabilities (e.g., OHT, AGV) to support larger packages and improve throughput, positioning the company for future share gains as the SLT market evolves.

# Investment concerns

1. Slower-than-expected AI demand: A potential slowdown in AI demand remains a key risk to Hon Precision’s growth outlook. As a significant portion of the

company’s future expansion is tied to AI-driven semiconductor demand, any deceleration in hyperscaler capex or delays in AI chip development cycles could translate into slower project ramps with key customers. This would not only impact near-term order visibility but could also push out qualification timelines for next-generation probe card solutions.

2. Stronger-than-expected market share gain from peers: Intensifying competition from global and regional peers also presents downside risk for Hon Precision. The handler market is competitive, with established international players and emerging

anced technologies. If competition   
15% sales CAGR.This kind of new test insertion will alsc   
321ssive 4- ing, technology advancements, or longer term. customer penetration – it could pressure Hon Precision’s market share, particularly in new AI-related projects where vendor qualification is highly competitive.

3. Slower-than-expected CPO development: Slower development in the CPOrelated equipment business may limit valuation upside. Although the equipment segment currently contributes a relatively small portion of revenue, it represents an important long-term optionality tied to next-generation interconnect technologies such as co-packaged optics. Any delays in Hon Precision’s progress or weaker-than-expected industry adoption of CPO could dampen investor expectations and cap further re-rating potential for the stock.

# Hon Precision: Financial Outlook

# Capacity and production

As of 4Q25, Hon Precision expanded its equipment capacity to 600 units. Currently, the company targets to expand at least $40 \%$ Y/Y by end-2026. With a view to meeting the very strong demand from AI and HPC customers, and as it takes some time for expansion, we expect its UTR to run at $> 1 0 0 \%$ in 2026-27e.

# Contract liabilities vs revenue

Hon Precision's contract liabilities rose $7 7 5 \%$ Y/Y in 4Q25, reaching $7 1 \%$ of revenue, indicating aggressive pre-payments from customers and solidifying future revenue streams, from which we expect strong profitability in the coming quarters.

# Revenue

The introduction of next-gen AI GPU (including NVIDIA, AMD and MediaTek) should drive a significant increase in the content value for testing, requiring longer testing time and more testing procedures needed, such as adding SLT into its process. Combined with the multiplier effect emerging in the CPO market, we expect these drivers to help Hon Precision’s revenue CAGR reach $6 7 \% ,$ , 2025-28e.

We expect Handler+ATC to continue to account for $7 5 \%$ of its total revenue throughout 2026-28e, with increasing adoption in tri-temp from areas such as auto and aerospace, and continual ASP increase of at least $1 5 . 2 0 \%$ annually in dualtemp, with spec migration acting as the key revenue driver. We also see new areas, such as CPO and micro-channel lid, acting as emerging opportunities for Hon Precision.

° We expect Coldplate to continue growing its revenue while overall mix remains 23- $2 5 \%$ in 2026-28e, as we see spec migration from installed base customers, and increasing site deployment in the SLT testing part.

# Gross profit

Hon Precision expanded its GM from $5 5 \%$ in 2024 to $5 6 . 5 \%$ in 2025, mainly driven by spec upgrade and better product mix from AI and HPC. We expect the continual spec migration trend, structurally longer testing time/procedure from large package size and emerging opportunities (such as CPO) to help lift Hon Precision's margin, which we expect to reach $6 \%$ in 2028e.

# EPS

# 添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17

Hon Precision's 2025 EPS was slightly constrained by the IPO (November 2025) employee stock option scheme, while it still managed to deliver $1 3 2 \%$ Y/Y growth.

Looking into 2026 and beyond, we expect overall currency policy (see our global team's latest report) to remain stable across the globe and no further stock issuance plan for the company. Thus, we expect to see operating leverage as high-margin business gradually bears fruit, with a $5 9 \%$ EPS CAGR, 2025-28e.

# Hon Precision: Our Estimates vs Consensus and What's Not Priced In

# We are more bullish than consensus on Hon Precision's 2026-27e earnings outlook

We think revenue momentum will remain strong in 2026-27e, as Hon Precision is a global 添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 leader in semiconductor automated test handler solutions, offering turnkey test design services to fabless/IDMs/system companies. We estimate Hon Precision has more than $70 \%$ volume market share within AI/HPC final test handlers. We think Hon Precision can capitalise on multiple growth drivers ahead in the leading-edge semi testing market for cloud AI and other applications. Drivers include: 1) expanding testing demand from robust cloud AI unit growth and longer testing times, 2) rising tool set value due to the need to handle larger packages with more complicated ATC, and 3) potential upside from SLT and CPO. Our 2026/2027 EPS estimates are $9 . 6 \% / 2 2 . 5 \%$ above consensus.

The market continues to view Hon Precision as a cyclical back-end semiconductor equipment supplier with only indirect AI exposure, when, in reality, AI is structurally shifting bottlenecks toward thermal handling, burn-in, and reliability testing, where it is directly leveraged; rising power density in AI chips is increasing test complexity, duration, and equipment intensity, creating non-linear demand (more tools, higher ASPs, and higher utilization per unit), while OSATs may be forced to pull forward capex to avoid 添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 bottlenecks, customer mix is quietly upgrading toward higher-margin advanced logic clients, and geopolitical supply chain duplication adds incremental demand – all combining to suggest a longer, more profitable, and less cyclical growth profile than currently assumed, with the critical underappreciated driver being increasing test time per AI chip, which amplifies revenue and earnings beyond simple volume growth.

# Bull/bear case scenarios

Our bull case scenario value is NT\$8,000: Stronger sales/earnings growth, with faster GM expansion

In this scenario we assume: 1) $100 \%$ revenue CAGR, 2025-28e, on stronger-than-expected adoption of tri-temp $\mathsf { A T C + }$ handler, with robust market share gain momentum in dual-temp ATC+handler and more market share gains; 2) faster-than-expected CPO introduction; 3) gross margin improves to over $7 0 \% ,$ , thanks to scale effects and the company's technology competitiveness; and 4) $> 1 5 0 \%$ earnings CAGR, 2025-28e.

Our bear case scenario value is NT\$2,500: Revenue/earnings growth is slower, with GM erosion.

In this scenario we assume: 1) $20 \%$ revenue CAGR, 2025-28e, on slower-than-expected adoption of tri-temp ATC+handler with robust market share gain momentum in dual-temp ATC+handler and no project wins in CPO development; 2) slower-than-expected CPO introduction; 3) customer bargaining power pushes gross margin below $4 0 \% ;$ and 4) $30 \%$ 3321Cad 04-17   
earnings CAGR, 2025-28e.
