# Hon Precision: Company Background

Established in April 2015 with the foundation of its predecessor Hon Tech (founded in 1999), Hon Precision specialises in turnkey service for automated handlers used for semiconductor testing. With a global footprint, it serves a variety of end-customers, ranging from smartphone processors, GPUs and ASICs, and the automotive, consumer, and memory segments. Other than its Taiwan headquarters, it has offices in the US, China, and Germany that help it fulfill its local and regional demand efficiently.

The management team has extensive experience within the semiconductor industry, as they all have engineering backgrounds, and each has several decades of experience within the semiconductor testing and testing equipment businesses. This in-depth experience allows Hon Precision to position itself as a trusted partner of OSAT and IC design companies.

Hon Precision engages directly with its end-customers, such as fabless companies or CSPs. Fabless companies such as smartphone application processor makers such as Apple and CSP/ASIC companies are involved in deciding and designing their own testing solutions and supply chains. In addition, Hon Precision also engages with leading OSAT customers such as ASE and KYEC for consumer and memory products. Hon Precision's end-customers can consign handlers to their own OSAT partners, making collaboration at the design stages crucial to secure future orders.

# Key product portfolio

添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 Hon Precision’s core business centers on semiconductor test handlers, offering a comprehensive portfolio of both standard and highly customized solutions tailored to diverse IC testing requirements and environments. The company’s revenue is primarily derived from three segments: 1) FT/SLT handlers; 2) active thermal control (ATC) systems – typically bundled with handlers; and 3) cold plates and related thermal components. Backed by its strong engineering capabilities and close customer collaboration, Hon Precision has established a leading position in high-performance computing, with over $70 \%$ market share in HPC FT handlers. Its product portfolio spans tri-temperature FT handlers, dual-temperature FT handlers, and SLT handlers, each addressing different endmarket requirements.

Tri-temp FT handlers represent the company’s highest-end offering, supporting a wide temperature range, from $- 7 0 ^ { \circ } \mathsf { C }$ to $+ 1 7 5 \mathrm { ^ \circ C } ,$ , primarily serving aerospace and automotive applications where extreme condition testing is required; these systems feature integrated ATC with patent-protected dry air technology to prevent condensation and ensure precise, 添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17stable thermal control.

Dual-temp FT handlers are the core volume driver, widely adopted by AI ASIC and GPU customers, and are capable of supporting up to 6kW thermal loads with multi-zone temperature control; the company is further advancing its capabilities through the development of MCL-integrated solutions to address next-generation power density requirements.

SLT handlers: In SLT, Hon Precision provides fully integrated handler systems with embedded test PCs and boards, combined with ATC and multi-site capabilities, positioning the company to capture growing demand as ${ \mathsf { A l / H P C } }$ and automotive customers increasingly adopt system-level validation.

![](images/c329649b368663a33b51c9d6880aaf21ef77a8c19fcde8c560977604d62c2b8d.jpg)  
Exhibit 103:Hon Precision's dual-temp and tri-temp handler solution   
Source: Hon Precision.

Cold plate: Complementing its handler offerings, Hon Precision’s cold plate solutions are designed to accommodate varying IC package sizes, architectures, and power profiles, enabling efficient heat dissipation during testing; these are offered both as part of bundled solutions and as standalone products, particularly for upgrade and replacement cycles. As chip complexity and power consumption continue to rise, cold plates – together with ATC – have become critical enablers of thermal stability during testing and are emerging as a meaningful incremental growth driver for the company.

# Key suppliers and customers

Hon Precision's key components include electromechanical and electronic components, transmission components, wiring, hardware, and material components, with no single supplier exceeding $10 \%$ or more of the company's total purchases.

Hon Precision’s customer base is dominated by AI- and HPC-related customers, which accounted for a combined $7 9 \%$ of 2M26 revenue. Hon Precision's key customers include NVIDIA, AMD, Broadcom, Marvell, Google, MediaTek, Apple, Amkor, KYEC, and ASE/SPIL, with US customers accounting for $5 7 \%$ . Currently, Taiwan accounts for $70 \%$ of its shipment location, as that's where most HPC and AI products are being tested.

# Key competitors

Hon Precision operates in a competitive landscape that includes major ATE and handler vendors such as Advantest, Teradyne, Chroma ATE, Cohu, Hangzhou Changchuan

Technology, and JHT, competing across both final test (FT) and system-level test (SLT) segments. Its primary competitors – Advantest (Japan), Teradyne (US), and Chroma (Taiwan) – are well-established global players; however, Hon Precision differentiates itself through its strong positioning in handlers, with an estimated $\sim 3 0 \%$ market share in general applications and over $70 \%$ in $\mathsf { A l / H P C }$ . The company benefits from its proximity to Taiwan’s semiconductor ecosystem, enabling efficient service to turnkey customers and OSATs, while its portfolio of more than 600 patents reinforces its technological moat. Hon Precision is also at the forefront of high-end handler development, including tritemperature systems and next-generation MCL-related solutions, and it works closely 添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 with customers on customized designs, allowing it to deliver competitive, tailored testing solutions that strengthen its leadership in advanced applications.

# MPI (6223.TWO): Key Beneficiary of the Larger Package Size; Tapping into CPO Testing; Initiate at OW

MPI offers a compelling combination of technology leadership and structural growth exposure. The company is well positioned in advanced probe card solutions, particularly in vertical probe cards (VPC) integrated with MEMS technology, which are increasingly 订阅研报添加微信 Lcritical for testing high-performance and fine-pitch semiconductor devices. This aligns closely with industry tailwinds driven by AI workloads, advanced process nodes, and the growing complexity of high-performance computing chips, all of which require higher density and superior signal integrity in testing.

In addition, MPI benefits from early-stage customer engagement through its equipment business, which enables deeper integration into clients’ development cycles and supports cross-selling opportunities. The company also has potential upside from emerging applications, such as co-packaged optics (CPO) and heterogeneous integration. With CPC and VPC business continuing to generate stable cash flow, MEMS probe cards continuing to scale its momentum, and the rising importance of high-frequency, high-density testing, MPI, in our view, is well 订阅研报添加微信positioned to deliver sustained growth and margin expansion. L

Earnings: Our 2027-28 EPS estimates are more bullish than consensus. We believe the market is underappreciating the durability and breadth of MPI’s growth drivers beyond the current AI narrative.

Valuation: We derive our price target from a residual income model. Our price target implies 44x 2027e P/E, which we think is justified given the $9 4 \%$ EPS CAGR that we project for 2025- 2028e. We believe valuation will continue to climb along with growing earnings.

Key downside risks include: 1) slower-than-expected AI demand, 2) slower-than-expected market share gain from peers, and 3) slower-than-expected CPO development.

# OVERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ We rate MPI OW relative to our coverage because it combines leading probe card technology (VPC/MEMS) with strong exposure to rising pin density and CPOrelated testing, positioning it to capture structural growth and share gains in advanced semiconductor testing. We expect MPI's revenue to grow at a $6 6 \%$ CAGR, 2025-2028e.

▪ MPI is among the top five global probe card suppliers and stands out as the only Taiwanese manufacturer capable of delivering a complete range of probe card solutions.

▪ Besides existing demand from R&D, we expect CPO-related testing equipment demand, including wafer-level and die-level testing, to be the key growth driver and account for $2 4 \%$ of 2028e total sales.

# 60x 2027e EPS

We assume: 1) $100 \%$ revenue CAGR, 2025- 28e, on stronger-than-expected adoption of MEMS probe cards, with robust market share gain momentum and more project wins in CPO development; 2) faster-thanexpected CPO introduction; 3) gross margin improves to over $7 0 \%$ , thanks to scale effects and the company's technology competitiveness; and 4) $> 1 5 0 \%$ EPS CAGR, 2025-28e.

# 44x 2028e EPS

We expect: 1) $6 6 \%$ revenue CAGR, 2025-28e, on stronger-than-expected adoption of MEMS probe cards, with robust market share gain momentum and more project wins in CPO development; 2) CPO-related revenue to bear fruit upon 2H27 introduction; 3) gross margin to reach $6 2 \%$ in 2028, thanks to scale effects and the company's technology competitiveness; and 4) $9 3 \%$ EPS CAGR, 2025-28e.

# 19x 2027e EPS

We assume: 1) $20 \%$ revenue CAGR, 2025- 28e, on slower-than-expected adoption of MEMS probe cards, with robust market share gain momentum and no project wins in CPO development; 2) slower-thanexpected CPO introduction; and 3) customer bargaining power pushes gross margin below $4 0 \%$ and $4 .$ ) $30 \%$ EPS CAGR, 2025- 28e.

# RISKS TO PT/RATING

# RISKS TO UPSIDE

Stronger-than-expected AI demand Stronger-than-expected market share gain from peers Stronger-than-expected CPO development

# RISKS TO DOWNSIDE

Slower-than-expected AI demand Slower-than-expected market share gain from peers Slower-than-expected CPO development

# MS ESTIMATES VS. CONSENSUS

![](images/1f9becc9ba4b33a27562fd5afd8a3ef8fbb897fd170ba73c99d6d870be2117ca.jpg)  
Source: Re nitiv, Morgan Stanley Research

1. Slower-than-expected AI demand: A potential slowdown in AI demand remains a key risk to MPI’s growth outlook. As a significant portion of the company’s future expansion is tied to AI-driven semiconductor demand, any deceleration in hyperscaler capex or delays in AI chip development cycles could translate into slower project ramps with key customers. This would not only impact near-term order visibility, but it could also push out qualification timelines for nextgeneration probe card solutions.

# 订阅研报添

2. Slower-than-expected market share gain from peers: Intensifying competition from global and regional peers also presents downside risk. The probe card 加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 market is highly competitive, with established international players and emerging regional suppliers continuously investing in advanced technologies. If competition becomes more aggressive – either through pricing, technology advancements, or customer penetration – it could pressure MPI’s market share, particularly in new AI-related projects where vendor qualification is highly competitive.

3. Slower-than-expected CPO development: Slower development in the CPOrelated equipment business may limit valuation upside. Although the equipment segment currently contributes a relatively small portion of revenue, it represents an important long-term optionality tied to next-generation interconnect technologies such as co-packaged optics. Any delays in MPI’s progress or weakerthan-expected industry adoption of CPO could dampen investor expectations and cap further re-rating potential for the stock.

# MPI: Financial Outlook

# Capacity

With a view to meeting the strong demand from customers, MPI plans to further increase its capacity substantially into 2026, in particular for MEMS probe cards. Currently, MPI plans to expand its VPC capacity from 1.2mn pin per month to 1.5mn pin per month in 2Q26, further reaching 1.8mn pin per month in 3Q26. For MEMS, the company plans to 添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 expand its capacity from 2.0mn pin per month to 2.5mn pin per month in 2Q26, and further reach 3.0mn pin per month in 3Q26, which should help it further expand its market share in the MEMS market.

# Order visibility and contract liabilities vs. revenue

The company currently has extended order visibility, and its robust demand is evident in its book-to-bill (BB) ratios: VPC is at a strong 1.93 in January 2026, with CPC also recovering to 1.63 January 2026 from 0.87 in December 2025. Management, during the 4Q25 earnings call, said that it also expects its $\mathtt { B / B }$ ratio for all product lines to stay above 1 during 2026. Additionally, contract liabilities rose $1 5 \%$ QoQ and $2 2 \%$ YoY in 4Q25, reaching $4 7 \%$ of revenue, indicating aggressive pre-payments from customers, helping to solidify future revenue streams.

# LCD123321Cad 04-17

# Revenue

After marking the eighth consecutive year of growth, at a $30 \% +$ Y/Y increase in 2025, the secular AI trend should, we expect, continue to drive MPI's revenue. We expect MPI's revenue to grow at a $6 6 \%$ CAGR, 2025-28e, mainly helped by improving mix in probe cards and increasing shipment in the equipment segment, mainly helped by CPO.

° Probe Cards: $70 \% +$ revenue, including vertical probe cards $\sim 4 0 \% +$ of total sales as of 2025), and rapidly rising contribution from MEMS probe cards $5 \%$ of 2025e total revenue) for AI chips. We expect MEMS probe cards will be the growth catalyst thanks to the strong AI chip growth trend, with MPI's revenue exposure increasing to $2 4 \%$ in 2028e.   
0 Equipment segment, including photonic automation, advanced semi test, and 加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 thermal system, accounted for 19% of 2025 total revenue (e.g., wafer probers used in R&D and engineering, automation modules, thermal chucks and environmental control systems). Besides the existing demand coming from R&D and applications such as LED and aerospace, we expect CPO-related testing demand, including wafer-level and die-level testing, to be the key growth driver for MPI's total revenue. We thus expect equipment to account for $2 4 \%$ of MPI's total revenue in   
2028e.

# Gross profit

MPI expanded its GM from $4 4 \%$ in 2019 to $56 \%$ in 2025, mainly driven by improving product mix from VPC and R&D investment in the equipment segment gradually bearing fruit.

We expect the increasing mix of high-margin VPC and MEMS products, and potentially more membrane adoption in the future – accounting for around $70 \% +$ of probe card revenue – to drive significant profit expansion, coupled with increasing equipment shipment, especially from CPO, to drive MPI's GM to $62 \%$ in 2028e.

# MPI: Our Estimates vs Consensus and What's Not Priced In

# We are more bullish than consensus on MPI's 2027-28e earnings outlook

We expect revenue momentum to remain strong in 2026-28e, thanks to continuous chip upgrades that lead to increasing pin counts in probe cards, with customers also further adopting MEMS probe cards, which leads to further ASP increase. Besides probe cards, we 添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 expect MPI to start making progress for its CPO products starting late 2026. On the gross margin front, continual migration to MEMS (GM at ${ > } 6 0 \% _ { 1 }$ , vs CPC/VPC at $4 5 / 5 0 \%$ ) and increasing equipment revenue should also help drive its corporate margin from $5 5 \%$ in $2 0 2 4$ to $6 2 \%$ in 2028e, per our projection. Our 2026/2027 EPS are $4 . 3 \% / 3 6 \%$ above consensus.

# What's not priced in?

We believe the market is underappreciating the durability and breadth of MPI’s growth drivers beyond the current AI narrative. While consensus increasingly factors in hyperscaler-driven AI ASIC demand, we believe that it does not fully capture the structural uplift from rising networking intensity and the associated increase in probe card pin counts, alongside accelerating customer migration toward MEMS. In addition, a cyclical recovery in non-AI segments (CPC/VPC) – partly driven by specification upgrades tied to higher pin counts – and MPI’s potential entry into the memory segment, where it could 添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 gain share from incumbents such as MJC, remain under-recognized. Finally, although MPI’s exposure to CPO wafer-level testing (Insertion 2) via its partnership with Advantest is relatively well understood, we see incremental upside from its early--stage exploration of die-level testing opportunities in Insertions 3 and 4, which we believe are not yet reflected in expectations.

Furthermore, we think there is still a catch-up opportunity in MPI's foreign holding positioning. QFII ownership has increased from $10 \%$ last August to $40 \%$ currently. We think this suggests that MPI’s long-term growth potential remains largely underdiscovered by global funds.

Our price target implies $4 4 \times / 2 4 \times 2 0 2 7 \mathrm { e } / 2 0 2 8 \mathrm { e } \mathsf { P } / \mathsf { E }$ . We believe its valuation should continue to climb along with growing earnings. We think its valuations align with 添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 other strong growth companies or companies that have an emerging business within our semiconductor coverage, such as Aspeed.

# Bull/bear case scenarios添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17

Our bull case scenario value is NT\$8,000: Stronger sales/EPS growth, with faster GM expansion

In this scenario we assume: 1) $100 \%$ revenue CAGR, 2025-28e, on stronger-than-expected adoption of MEMS probe cards with robust market share gain momentum and more project wins in CPO development; 2) faster-than-expected CPO introduction; 3) gross margin improves to over $70 \%$ thanks to scale effects and the company's technology competitiveness; and 4) $> 1 5 0 \%$ EPS CAGR, 2025-228e.

Our bear case scenario value is NT\$2,500: Revenue/EPS growth is slower, with GM erosion.

In this scenario we assume: 1) $20 \%$ revenue CAGR, 2025-28e, on slower-than-expected adoption of MEMS probe cards, with robust market share gain momentum and no project wins in CPO development; 2) slower-than-expected CPO introduction; 3) gross margin 添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 falls below 40% due to the high bargaining power of customers; and 4) $30 \%$ EPS CAGR, 2025-28e.

# Peer comparison and valuation

We expect MPI's EPS to grow at a $9 4 \%$ CAGR, 2025-28e, or a $76 \%$ CAGR, 2024-27e, much faster than the $2 6 \%$ peer average for 2024-27e (per consensus forecasts), thanks to its leadership in the US AI ASIC supply chain and its continual efforts to penetrate into CPO insertion testing. Although the stock currently trades at 71x/34x 2026e/2027e P/E, we still expect it to further trend up, as we believe that we are still at the halfway point for the market to capitalize on its structural earnings growth potential for AI and CPO. MPI's multiple is lower than that of competitors, such as Technoprobe/Formfactor, at 46x/57x 2027e P/E (based on consensus forecasts), but we think it's likely to catch up, given its leading position in AI ASIC and also as it is scaling revenue from its MEMS probe card business. Smaller Taiwanese probe card peers, such as KSMT and Inno-star, are also trading at higher multiples, at 99x and 97x 2027e P/E (based on consensus forecasts), but remain focused on single ASIC projects.

# MPI: Company Background and Overview

Founded in 1995 in Hsinchu, Taiwan, MPI Corporation is aiming to build precision probing systems for the Taiwan semiconductor testing market. In its early years, the company focused primarily on engineering probers for laboratory and wafer-level characterization – a niche but vital segment that supported IC design companies and research institutes. MPI’s precision mechanical know-how positioned it as one of the earliest domestic suppliers capable of producing high-accuracy probe stations locally, vs. the competitors 添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 commonly in Japan or the US. By the early 2000s, MPI had leveraged its precision mechanical base to move up the value chain into vertical probe cards, targeting the testing of DRAM and logic devices. MPI also established its in-house PCB processing and substrate assembly lines to ensure quality control and shorten turnaround time. Over time, MPI has become a key local partner for leading OSATs, supporting vertical probe cards used for volume production in memory and SoC testing.

As TSMC developed its CoWoS and advanced packaging since the 2010s, wafer-level test complexity grew substantially. MPI made a decisive investment in MEMS probe-card fabrication for sub-50um pitches or the multi-ampere current densities required by more advanced SoC. It developed proprietary process for micro-spring and thinfilm formation and transformed MPI from a precision-mechanical manufacturer into a microelectromechanical device company – directly competing with global leaders, such as FormFactor and Technoprobe, in the most technology-intensive segment of the testing interface market.

# Probe cards

Cantilever probe cards are one of the earliest and most established wafer probing technologies, consisting of metallic probe needles that extend horizontally from the probe card substrate to contact the bond pads on a semiconductor wafer. These probes are typically made from materials such as tungsten or beryllium copper and are individually formed and aligned to match the pad layout of the device under test. Cantilever probe cards are generally cost-effective and are well suited for low- to mediumpin-count applications, including analog, discrete, and some mixed-signal devices. However, as device geometries shrink and pad densities increase, maintaining precise alignment, consistent contact force, and planarity across a large number of probes becomes more difficult, limiting their suitability for advanced, high-density semiconductor testing.

Vertical probe cards were developed to address the limitations of cantilever designs, particularly for high-pin-count and fine-pitch applications. In vertical probe cards, probes are arranged perpendicular to the wafer surface and often use compliant structures such as spring pins or buckling beams to maintain consistent contact force. This vertical architecture improves planarity and enables more uniform electrical contact across all test points, which is critical for advanced integrated circuits such as DRAM and highperformance logic devices. Vertical probe cards also support higher levels of parallel testing, allowing multiple dies to be tested simultaneously and improving overall throughput. While they offer enhanced performance and scalability, they are more complex to manufacture and typically come at a higher cost compared to cantilever probe cards.

订阅研报添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 MEMS (micro-electro-mechanical systems) probe cards represent a more advanced generation of probing technology, utilizing semiconductor fabrication techniques such as photolithography and thin-film processing to create highly precise probe structures. These probe cards feature microfabricated spring elements and contact tips that enable extremely fine pitch capability and high uniformity across thousands of contact points. MEMS probe cards are particularly well suited for advanced semiconductor nodes, waferlevel testing, and applications requiring high-frequency performance and low contact resistance. Their batch fabrication approach allows for high repeatability and scalability, although the initial development costs and manufacturing complexity are relatively high.

As a result, MEMS probe cards are primarily used in high-volume production environments and cutting-edge semiconductor applications.

MPI provides omnidirectional products to global customers, including fine pitch CPC, highspeed VPC and low-force MEMS solutions. As one of the top five global probe card suppliers, MPI is the only Taiwanese manufacturer capable of producing a full range of probe cards, including cantilever (CPC), vertical (VPC), and MEMS-based solutions. This comprehensive technology portfolio addresses high-frequency and fine-pitch testing needs, establishing a strong competitive moat. The company holds the leading market share among Taiwanese VPC manufacturers, and its high-end MEMS probe cards are being integrated into the supply chains of major international clients, with volume production expected after validation in 2025.

In probe cards, VPC describes the probe's physical orientation (vertical), while MEMS refers to the advanced microfabrication technology used to create the tiny, precise probes, often integrated into VPCs for high-density testing (vertical MEMS probe cards) for complex logic chips, offering superior signal integrity and fine pitch compared to traditional cantilever cards. With AI chips moving to more advanced nodes and complex designs, the most advanced, high-density VPCs are likely to increasingly incorporate MEMS technology for their probes, leading to superior performance.

# Thermal and Advanced Semiconductor Test (AST) Equipment

MPI's thermal products are designed to conduct reliability tests for chips operating in extreme environmental conditions, (such as those used in auto, EV, aerospace, and telecommunication industries. The company says that these can reach 300 degrees from - 100 degrees within 10 seconds.

添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 MPI Photonics’ automation equipment is differentiated by its innovative temperature control systems, which are designed to meet increasingly stringent testing requirements. Through ongoing R&D investment in platform development and system enhancements, MPI has built a portfolio of patented thermal technologies that deliver high efficiency and energy savings. These solutions not only improve test performance and stability but also help customers meet their ESG goals by reducing overall energy consumption.

A key competitive advantage for MPI lies in its highly skilled R&D team. The company’s thermal solutions are developed by industry veterans with over 100 years of combined experience, enabling deep technical expertise and a strong understanding of customer pain points. This level of domain knowledge supports the development of reliable, highprecision systems tailored to advanced photonics and semiconductor testing needs.

Furthermore, MPI benefits from deep collaboration with leading global customers across both engineering validation and high-volume production. These partnerships allow MPI to co-develop solutions that are closely aligned with real-world application requirements, accelerating time-to-market and improving product relevance. As a result, MPI’s automation equipment has successfully expanded into a wide range of high-growth applications, including automotive, 5G/RF communications, fiber optics, and sensing technologies.

In 2026, MPI merged its AST equipment with thermal applications to create synergy on product development. Originally, AST equipment had been chip probers used in the R&D/ design stage and mainly sold to IC designers. Owing to MPI's skilled technology in the optical field, it has successfully designed CPO wafer-level testing equipment and die-to-die probers. According to our supply chain check, MPI has started shipping such equipment to OSAT and foundry customers for COUPE and CPO packaging and testing, with CPO revenue likely to start contributing in 2H26.

MPI AST equipment benefits from unique global positioning, combining advanced analytical probing solutions with strong RF measurement core technologies. This integrated capability enables MPI to provide comprehensive solutions for high-frequency response measurement, positioning the company as a leading provider across the full 添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17spectrum of RF and mmWave testing applications.

Another key strength is MPI’s customer-centric design approach. By incorporating Voice of the Customer (VOC) into its development process, MPI ensures that its systems are closely aligned with real-world requirements, addressing specific pain points and improving usability, performance, and overall customer satisfaction. This approach enhances product relevance and strengthens long-term customer relationships.

In addition, MPI offers a complete and diversified solution portfolio that supports a wide range of applications. Its product lineup covers critical testing needs, including device modeling, RF and mmWave, wafer-level reliability (WLR), high-power testing, failure analysis, and extreme temperature environments. This broad capability allows customers to rely on MPI as a one-stop solution provider, reducing integration complexity and improving operational efficiency.

# Key competitors

Collectively, the probe card market exhibits high concentration and significant 添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 technological barriers. The top players – FormFactor, Technoprobe, MJC, Nidec SV TCL and MPI – along with a handful of others, command the majority of the AI chip probe card market.

Key differentiators among these players include: 1) Technology leadership: MEMS probe card precision at fine pitches drives testing for cutting-edge chips. 2) Application focus: Logic and advanced packaging require high-density cards; memory and automotive rely on durability. 3) Regional reach and service: Proximity to fabs and OSATs significantly affects adoption and support cycles.

In this landscape, FormFactor leads globally with MEMS innovation and scale, Technoprobe offers broad vertical expertise with high volume, MJC holds a strong regional niche with precision materials, and MPI punches above its weight with Taiwan-centric agility and specialized test solutions.

For the equipment side, MPI's key competitors in wafer prober include OPTO Tech, Fittech, Chroma ATE, Cascade, Hauman Technologies, WeiMin Industrial and SIDEA. For VCSEL testing systems, its competitors include BOST　and Feedlitech in China.

# Key suppliers and customers

A probe card consists of three components — probe head (pin), multi-layer organic (MLO), and printed circuit board (PCB). MPI already has the capability to self-design and manufacture probe heads and MLO. Nevertheless, since August 2024, MPI has been building a new PCB manufacturing facility in Hukou, Hsinchu, with the goal of increasing its in-house PCB production ratio to $50 \%$ (vs. ${ \tt< } 1 0 \%$ previously) by 4Q26. The new PCB

facility is dedicated to manufacturing high-end tailor-made PCBs (up to 70-80 layers). Once PCB capacity successfully ramps up in 2027, MPI will be able to enhance probe card quality, enable a higher degree of customization, and significantly shorten production lead time. The current production lead time is 12 weeks (8 weeks for PCB) and can increase up to 6 months during peak times, such as in 2024 when AI demand far exceeded supply. With the ability to self-produce high-end PCB, MPI can shorten production lead time to 8 weeks (around $2 / 3$ of the original time) vs. overseas peers’ lead time of at least 12 weeks, gaining a paramount time advantage and flexibility over competitors, especially amid the trend of shorter time to market.

MPI’s customers base is dominated by AI ASIC and CoWoS ecosystem leaders, including Broadcom, Marvell, TSMC and ASE/SPIL. For CPC business, it's largely dominated by DDIC-related customers, including Novatek. MPI has the advantage of regional service networks when customers require local refurbishment within hours, compared to its global peers that do not have this capability, such as like FormFactor and Technoprobe.

# WinWay (6515.TW): Socket Leader Leveraging AI Packaging Complexity; Initiate at OW

WinWay is the world’s 2nd-largest socket provider, with test sockets/burn-in sockets contributing $4 3 \% / 9 \%$ of its 2025 revenue. We expect WinWay’s revenue to grow $8 9 \% / 6 6 \% / 5 9 \%$ during 2026e/2027e/2028e, or at a $7 7 \%$ revenue CAGR, 2025-2028e,       ‹¢–xb¥mûR _®Omainly driven by: 1) HPC testing leader leveraging SLT expansion and AI ASIC momentum; 2) hypersocket innovation driving structural ASP upside; and 3) vertical integration and MEMS expansion on track, benefitting from higher pin counts and spec migration. We view WinWay as a key enabler for reducing lead times, strengthening supply chain control, and supporting margin expansion, with a clear pathway toward achieving a $50 \%$ gross margin profile over time.

Earnings: We think revenue momentum will remain strong in 2026-28e, driven by continuous chip upgrades that lead to increasing pin counts in coaxial sockets, with customers also further adopting the hypersocket, which fosters further ASP increase. We think consensus is not pricing in the magnitude and durability of WinWay’s margin and earnings expansion, driven by   structural product mix upgrades. ‹¢–xb¥mûR _®O

Valuation: We derive our price target from a residual income model. Our price target implies 61x/35x 2027e/2028e P/E, vs WinWay's EPS CAGR of $9 5 \%$ in 2026-28e. We believe its valuation will continue to climb along with growing earnings.

Key downside risks include: 1) slower-than-expected adoption of hypersockets and MEMS probe cards, 2) slower-than-expected SLT and burn-in introduction, and 3) no edge AI demand in the next few years.

# WinWay: Investment Positives and Concerns

Investment positives

HPC Testing Leader leveraging SLT Expansion and AI ASIC Momentum: SLT has evolved from a supplementary yield check into a core requirement in the AI era, as rising chip complexity requires validation under real-world operating conditions. Unlike traditional FT, which captures electrical performance within seconds, SLT 微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 runs workload scenarios over much longer periods – often from tens of minutes to several hours. This shift materially increases test intensity and duration, expanding the TAM for test sockets by an estimated 4-5x, based on company guidance. WinWay’s leadership in HPC testing rests on its long-standing partnership with several US AI GPU customer, where it acts as a core supplier for advanced GPU testing solutions. At the same time, the company has successfully expanded into the US AI ASIC ecosystem, securing high-margin FT and SLT socket orders that should form a meaningful second growth driver. We see WinWay’s competitive position supported by deep co-development relationships with leading chipmakers, strong simulation and engineering capabilities to meet demanding specifications, and close proximity to Taiwan’s semiconductor manufacturing ecosystem, which together support further share gains across additional ASIC platforms.

2. Hypersocket Innovation Driving Structural ASP Upside: Ongoing chip upgrades continue to raise pin counts, driving higher socket pricing. Average pin counts per 微信 socket in the AI/HPC segment stand at around $5 . 7  { \mathrm { k } }$ pins (including AI ASICs), vs 1k $^ +$ for consumer segments such as smartphones and PCs. As next-generation AI platforms scale toward ${ 1 0 , 0 0 0 + }$ pins and larger package sizes, socket design plays a more critical role in ensuring both electrical and mechanical performance. WinWay’s proprietary hypersocket technology has emerged as a leading solution to address package warpage and extreme thermal design power (TDP), supported by ongoing collaboration and qualification with key AI GPU and ASIC customers. Its composite architecture improves signal integrity and structural stability, which we believe supports continued ASP expansion. Meanwhile, the company is expanding its in-house spring probe capacity for test sockets to 9mn pins per month by late 2026. We view this expansion as a key enabler for shorter lead times, stronger supply chain control, and margin improvement, with a clear pathway toward a $50 \%$ gross margin profile over time.

3. Vertical Integration and MEMS Expansion on Track, Benefitting from Higher 加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 Pin Counts and Spec Migration: Leveraging its close relationship with NVIDIA, WinWay has expanded into NVIDIA’s gaming GPU and Grace CPU supply chain already, which primarily uses VPC for chip probing. At the same time, WinWay continues to advance its vertical integration strategy and MEMS-related initiatives. Its partnership with Technoprobe for MEMS probe cards has progressed into Phase 2 mass production, with revenue contribution expected to scale meaningfully from earlier stages. We expect WinWay to start supplying NVIDIA's AI GPU, supported by a broader outsourcing trend among leading customers.

# WinWay: Financial Outlook

# Capacity

WinWay is expanding its in-house spring probe capacity for test sockets to 9mn pins per month by late 2026. This expansion should further support further scaling of $5 \boldsymbol { \mathrm { k } }$ -pinsocket, with monthly output rising from $2 \boldsymbol { \mathrm { k } }$ to $4 . 2 k$ per month by end-2026. Vertical integration also gives WinWay greater control over production lead time, shortening them 添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 relative to an outsourced model and enabling a more agile response to AI NPI cycles. From a financial perspective, doubling in-house capacity should support GM sustainability above $45 \%$ and, in our view, provide a path to the $50 \%$ level, as sockets using in-house pins typically deliver higher margins than those relying on outsourced pins.

# Contract liabilities vs revenue

WinWay's contract liabilities rose $40 \%$ Q/Q and $4 6 \%$ YoY in 4Q25, reaching $12 \%$ of revenue. This increase reflects stronger customer pre-payments and improves visibility into future revenue streams. In our view, this trend supports the outlook for profitability in the coming quarters.

# Revenue

The introduction of next-generation AI GPUs (including NVIDIA, AMD, and MediaTek) should drive a meaningful increase in test-socket content value. Spec upgrades are pushing pin counts above ${ \mathsf { 1 0 k } } + ,$ while a growing number of customers are adopting hypersockets, which carry higher ${ \sf A S P S }$ than coaxial sockets. Together with the emerging multiplier effect in the SLT market, these factors should support our expectation that WinWay’s revenue will deliver a $7 1 \%$ CAGR, 2025-28e.

We expect sockets to continue to account for $\sim 7 0 \%$ of WinWay's total revenue over 2026-28e. Within this segment, hypersockets should keep gaining share from coaxial socket, as customers increasingly migrate to this product given its advantages over traditional coaxial and plastic sockets, including easier maintenance and higer reliability. Hypersockets previously contributed a very low single-digit $\%$ of WinWay's total revenue, but we expect to scale to at least $5 . 7 0 \%$ by end-2026e. Demand for other products, such as burn-in sockets, is also rising, as a growing number of chips – including CPUs and GPUs – now require burn-in testing.

° We expect probe cards to continue delivering revenue growth, while maintaining an overall mix of ${ \sim } 2 7 \%$ in 2026-28e. Revenue contributions should increasingly come from VPC and MEMS, particularly from NVIDIA. Beyond supplying probe cards for NVIDIA's gaming GPUs and CPUs, we believe WinWay's partnership with Technoprobe on MEMS probe cards has progressed into Phase 2 mass production, which positions the company to expand into the AI GPU segment.

# Gross profit

We look for WinWay to expand its GM from $40 \%$ in 2018 to $45 \%$ in 2026e, driven primarily by an improving product mix away from coaxial sockets and as R&D investments in the probe card segment gradually begin to bear fruit.

We expect the rising product mix of hypersockets, higher-end burn-in sockets, and MEMS probe card products to support further margin expansion. On this basis, we forecast WinWay's GM to rise from $3 7 \%$ in 2023 to $54 \%$ in 2028e.

# EPS

WinWay's 2025 EPS saw a modest impact from unfavorable FX movements in 2Q25 and a slightly weaker mix in plastic/RF sockets. Despite these headwinds, the company still delivered 40% Y/Y EPS growth, following a strong $1 5 6 \%$ Y/Y expansion in 2024.

Looking into 2026 and beyond, we expect global currency policy to remain stable. (See our global team's latest report.) As a result, we expect operating leverage to strengthen as high-margin businesses continue to scale, supporting a $9 5 \%$ EPS CAGR, 2025-28e.

# WinWay: Valuation Methodology and Peer Comparison

We assume a $9 . 1 4 \%$ cost of equity (risk-free rate $2 . 0 \%$ and risk premium $6 . 0 \%$ ), a payout ratio of $8 0 \% ,$ a medium-term growth rate of $1 6 . 4 \% ,$ and a terminal growth rate of $4 . 5 \%$ . Our price target implies $6 1 \times / 3 5 \times 2 0 2 7 { \mathrm { e } } / 2 0 2 8 { \mathrm { e } } \mathsf { P } / \mathsf { E } ,$ vs WinWay's EPS CAGR of $9 6 \%$ in 2026-28e. We believe its valuation should continue to climb along with growing 添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 earnings. We think its valuations align with other structural growth companies, such as Aspeed.

# Bull/bear case scenarios

Our bull case scenario value is NT\$15,000: Stronger revenue/EPS growth, with faster GM expansion

In this scenario we assume: 1) $100 \%$ revenue CAGR, 2025-28e, on stronger-than-expected adoption of hypersockets and MEMS probe cards with robust market share gain momentum; 2) faster-than-expected SLT and burn-in socket introduction to its endcustomers; 3) gross margin improves to over $7 0 \% _ { \ i }$ thanks to scale effects and the company's technology competitiveness; and 4) $> 1 5 0 \%$ EPS CAGR, 2025-28e.

Our bear case scenario value is NT\$9,000: Slower revenue/EPS growth, with GM erosion.

In this scenario we assume: 1) $30 \%$ revenue CAGR, 2025-28e, on slower-than-expected adoption of hypersockets and MEMS probe cards; 2) slower-than-expected SLT and burnin socket introduction to its end-customers; 3) customer bargaining power pushes gross 添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 margin below 40%; and 4) 50% EPS CAGR, 2025-28e.

# Peer comparison and valuation

We expect WinWay's EPS to rise at a $9 5 \%$ CAGR, 2025-28e or an $80 \%$ CAGR, 2024-27e, we above the peer average of a $2 6 \%$ CAGR, 2024-27e (based on consensus forecasts). This outperformance reflects WinWay's leadership in the US AI GPU supply chain and its core capabilities in addressing 224G/3,000W signal and thermal bottlenecks, where WinWay leads at the 3,000W spec. Although the stock currently trades at 82x/44x/25x

2026e/2027e/2028e P/E, we still expect further upside. In our view, the market remains only midway through capitalizing on WinWay's structural earnings growth potential and the SLT volume multiplier effect, alongside emerging opportunities such as CPO, where WinWay serves as a critical enabler across the insertion testing process. By comparison, testing socket peers Leeno and ISC are both trading at 40x 2027e P/E (based on consensus forecasts), with greater exposure to consumer technology and smaller AI GPU/ ASIC projects, and slower growth than WinWay. Smaller Taiwanese probe card peers, such as KSMT and Inno-star are also trading at respective 97x and 94x 2027e P/Es (based on

believe the current cycle represents a paradigm shift rather than a cyclical peak, supported by structural AI demand. # WinWay: Company Background and Overview

Founded in 2001 and headquartered in Kaohsiung, Taiwan, WinWay Technology is a leading provider of semiconductor test-interface solutions, specializing in high-frequency and high-speed applications for global IC design houses and OSAT customers.

The company focuses on developing and manufacturing high-performance test sockets and probe cards, which serves as critical components in semiconductor testing. Supported by strong in-house R&D capabilities and a high degree of customization, WinWay has built a leading market position and ranks as the world’s largest test socket supplier by revenue, according to Yole Group.

We believe WinWay is well positioned to benefit from rising testing complexity associated with AI, high-performance computing, and advanced packaging trends. Its competitive strengths include advanced test interface technology, a broad product portfolio, and a global customer support network, which support its position in next-generation semiconductor testing.

# Key product lines

and $4 \%$ from others. Looking ahead, we expect WinWay to further scale revenue across   
both segments, with sockets reaching $7 1 \%$ of total revenue and probe cards contributing . 微信 LCD123321 ad 4

# Probe cards

As WinWay's core focus remains on the socket market, it has expanded into probe cards through a strategic alliance with Technoprobe rather than pursuing in-house production, 添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 unlike peers such as CHPT and MPI. Phase 1 of the partnership centered on a multi-year technical alignment and the localized integration of Technoprobe’s flagship MEMS probe head technology. This phase included comprehensive process verification and the buildout of a service ecosystem within Taiwan’s OSAT cluster. While the initial procurement of high-cost MEMS components weighed on WinWay’s gross margin as revenue contribution began in 3Q25, Phase 1 met its primary objective of securing design-ins from US GPU customers, including NVIDIA. This outcome demonstrated WinWay's ability to meet the stringent requirements of advanced-node testing.

production cycle, supported by a minimum 5-year long-term supply agreement. This phase features materially larger contract values and shipment volumes, which we expect to be several times larger than those achieved in Phase 1. By combining Technoprobe’s worldleading MEMS technology with WinWay's rapid-response technical support, WinWay has evolved from a specialized test socket supplier into a provider of end-to-end testing solutions, covering chip probing, FT, and SLT. We expect the probe card business, supported by the Phase 2 scale-up, to expand WinWay's probe card revenue from NT $\$ 23b{ \mathrm { b } }$ in 2025 to NT\$10bn in 2028e, accounting for $2 7 \%$ of total revenue in 2028e.

How does WinWay's probe card business differ from MPI's? Although WinWay is ramping up its probe card business, its business model differs from MPI’s. WinWay primarily sources Technoprobe and other third-party components for probe cards, while MPI maintains a higher proportion of in-house component manufacturing and is further pursuing $100 \%$ in-house in the long run. We view probe cards as a complementary element that completes WinWay’s broader testing solution for clients, spanning Chip probing, FT, SLT, and burn-in, rather than a standalone growth driver.

# Sockets

Testing sockets serve as critical enabling components in semiconductor testing, including FT, burn-in, and SLT, by providing a temporary yet high-performance electrical interface between the device under test (DUT) and automated test equipment (ATE). Their core role is to maintain signal integrity, mechanical alignment, and thermal stability throughout the testing cycle, directly influencing test yield, throughput, and overall cost efficiency. As 添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 semiconductor complexity increases – particularly in high-performance computing (HPC), AI, and advanced connectivity – socket performance requirements have tightened, reinforcing their importance within the back-end testing ecosystem.

Plastic/RF sockets and coaxial sockets address distinct performance requirements across frequency ranges and cost structures. Plastic sockets, which see broad adoption in highvolume manufacturing, provide a cost-efficient solution with sufficient electrical   
performance for general-purpose IC testing, primarily for consumer grade and   
mainstream products. RF sockets represent a higher spec subset, designed with controlled impedance and low insertion loss to support reliable testing of   
high frequency devices such as mobile RF front end modules and connectivity chips. Coaxial sockets occupy the high end segment, using coaxial transmission structures to deliver superior signal integrity, minimal crosstalk, and low return loss at very high frequencies, including mmWave and high speed digital interfaces. As semiconductor designs push performance limits, demand for coaxial sockets has increased in applications where precision and signal fidelity are critical, such as AI and HPC chips, despite their 123321Cad 04-   
higher price point.

Hypersockets represent a next-generation testing socket architecture designed for highend applications such as AI accelerators, GPUs, and chiplet-based designs. Key differentiators include ultra-fine pitch capability, scalability support for high pin counts, and enhanced signal and power integrity, which address the limitations of conventional sockets at advanced nodes. Hypersockets also offer easier maintenance, as customers can directly replace the conductive epoxy. While adoption remains at an early stage, hypersockets command structurally higher ASPs and margin profiles, positioning them as a potential long-term growth driver alongside rising semiconductor complexity and heterogeneous integration. We expect hypersockets to contribute at least $5 . 7 0 \%$ of total revenue by end-2026e, acting as a key growth driver for the company.

Burn-in sockets are structurally and functionally differentiated by their focus on reliability screening rather than pure signal performance. These sockets maintain stable electrical contact under prolonged high-temperature and high-voltage conditions, typically during burn-in processes designed to identify early-life device failures. Material selection and mechanical design emphasize thermal endurance, contact durability, and resistance to oxidation or deformation over extended test cycles. While burn-in sockets generally carry lower unit pricing than high-frequency sockets, demand closely tracks overall semiconductor production volumes and reliability requirements, supporting a stable, volume-driven segment within the testing socket market. We also expect broader customer adoption of burn-in steps in testing flows. NVIDIA and AMD have incorporated burn-in testing across multiple product generations, and we expect MediaTek's Google TPU Zebrafish platform to introduce burn-in testing in 2H26.

# Key competitors

Collectively, the probe card market is highly concentrated and is characterized by significant technological barriers. Leading players – FormFactor, Technoprobe, MJC, Nidec, and MPI – along with a small group of others, account for the majority of the AI chip probe card market. Within this competitive landscape, FormFactor leads globally through MEMS innovation and scale; Technoprobe combines broad vertical expertise with highvolume manufacturing; MJC maintains a strong regional niche supported by precision materials capabilities; and MPI leverages Taiwan-centric agility and specialized test solutions, with primary exposure to ASIC and networking applications. For WinWay, its end to end testing solution offering and close relationship with NVIDIA have enabled initial penetration into gaming GPU and Vera CPU probing. We expect this footprint to 添加微信 LCD123321Cad 04-17 expand into AI GPU as TSMC is outsources its chip probing requirements.

Overall, the socket market remains highly fragmented, with the largest player accounting for just $10 \%$ of total market share. WinWay’s market share has increased from $5 . 8 \%$ in 2022 to $8 . 6 \%$ in $2 0 2 4$ , making it the second-largest supplier of test and burn-in sockets, driven by accelerating AI demand for high-end test sockets, according to Yole. Smiths, Yamaichi, Enplas, LEENO and Cohu remain WinWay's key competitors.

# Key suppliers and customers

A probe card consists of three components: probe head (pins), the multi-layer organic (MLO) substrate, and printed circuit board (PCB). MPI has in-house capabilities to design and manufacture both probe heads and MLO. By contrast, WinWay primarily sources probe heads and pins from Technoprobe, with related purchases accounting for a stable $\sim 3 0 \%$ of total procurement in recent years. In test sockets, WinWay relies mainly on inhouse production, supported by ongoing capacity expansion plans over the next few years.

WinWay’s customer base is concentrated among leading AI GPU and HPC players that serve as the primary users of TSMC's advanced nodes. As of 2025, NVIDIA accounts for $40 \%$ of revenue, followed by AMD at $2 0 \% ,$ Broadcom at $1 0 \% ,$ and MediaTek at $1 0 \% ,$ making them WinWay’s top 4 customers. Geographically, WinWay’s revenue exposure skews toward North America, which represented $67 \%$ of total revenue in 2025, followed by Taiwan at $1 5 \%$ and China at $1 3 \%$ .

# Appendix: Measuring the Global Semiconductor Assembly and Test Services Market Size

The global semiconductor assembly and test services (SATS) market is undergoing a significant transformation, shifting from a volume-driven industry toward a more strategic role in enabling next-generation technologies. This transition reflects a clear structural bifurcation within the market, with growth increasingly driven by advanced packaging b¥mûR _®Oá       L C D 1 2 3 3 2 1 C a d       0 4 - 1 7solutions rather than conventional, high volume assembly and test services.

According to Gartner, the total semiconductor packaging and testing services market is projected to reach US\$122.6bn by $2 0 2 9 \mathsf { e } ,$ , implying a five-year (2024-29e) CAGR of $8 . 3 \%$ . The outsourced segment of the SATS market is forecast to grow from $\cup S \$ 42.26n$ in 2024 to $\cup 5 \$ 65.26n$ in 2029e, a CAGR of $9 . 1 \%$ . This growth reflects the rising dependence of fabless companies and integrated device manufacturers (IDMs) on specialized partners to support increasingly complex packaging requirements and higher capital intensity.

The most important development in the SATS market is the accelerating divergence between advanced and traditional packaging. Advanced packaging is forecast by Gartner to expand from $\cup \ S \$ 40$ .4bn in $2 0 2 4$ to $\mathsf { U S S 7 3 . 6 b n }$ in $2 0 2 9 \Theta$ , or a CAGR of $1 2 . 7 \%$ . This segment showed notable resilience during the market-wide stagnation in 2023, suggesting that its demand drivers are less exposed to short-term cyclical downturns. In 2026, advanced packaging is expected by Gartner to grow $1 4 . 5 \%$ to $\cup 5 \$ 453.8$ bn. By 2029, advanced packaging is set to account for the majority of total market value, signaling a ¥mûR _®Oá       L C D 1 2 3 3 2 1 C a d       0structural reshaping of the semiconductor packaging and testing industry.

The growing computational demands of AI and HPC have emerged as the primary catalyst. As traditional transistor scaling under Moore’s Law slows, advanced packaging has become a key avenue for performance gains by enabling heterogeneous integration of specialized chiplets into a single system-in-package (SiP). This approach plays a critical role in addressing the memory bandwidth bottleneck in AI accelerators, where 3D-stacked HBM is co-packaged with GPUs on a 2.5D silicon interposer.

In particular, 2.5D/3D packaging is projected by Gartner to grow from US\$8.98bn in 2024 to $\mathsf { U S S } \$ 16.38 b \mathsf { n }$ by 2029e, implying a $1 2 . 8 \%$ CAGR. This rapid expansion is driven primarily by demand for HBM stacks, which are essential for AI and HPC accelerators. 2.5D/3D packaging provides the critical integration platform – namely the silicon interposer – that links 3D-stacked HBM stacks to high-performance logic chips, positioning this segment as a direct beneficiary of the AI boom.

‹¢–xb¥mûR _®Oá       L C D 1 2 3 3 2 1 C a d       0 4 - 1 7 Wafer-level packaging (WLP) represents another major growth driver and delivers the largest absolute dollar expansion. Gartner expects the segment to grow from US\$13.3bn in 2024 to $\mathsf { U S S 2 9 . 9 b r }$ in 2029e, implying a $1 7 . 7 \%$ CAGR. WLP plays a critical role in enabling miniaturization and higher levels of integration across smartphones, wearables, and Internet of Things (IoT) devices.

In contrast, Gartner expects traditional packaging to grow at a slower pace, expanding from US\$41.9bn in 2024 to $\mathsf { U S S } 4 8 . 9 6 \mathsf { n }$ in 2029e, implying a CAGR of $3 . 1 \%$ . This more modest growth reflects the continued commoditization of legacy technologies such as wire bonding, which face displacement from more advanced packaging solutions. The wide 118 disparity in growth rates underscores that the majority of the industry’s future growth and value creation will concentrate in advanced packaging.
